L707: ## C021 TEAE
L708: The preferred terms “diplopia”, “nystagmus”, “visual impairment” and “vision blurred” in study C021 are examined for time to onset, severity, SAEs, and discontinuation, Table 39. There were 174 adverse events in this pool from 154 patients. None were recorded as serious adverse events. The mean and median time to onset of this adverse event pool (that may affect vision) were 124 days and 89 days respectively. Eighty one percent (81%) of these events were judged as “mild” by the investigator with 18% judged as “moderate” severity and 1% judged as “severe”
L709: There were 3 cenobamate discontinuations from 4 patients in with entries in this group of adverse events. One of these events was “vision blurred” the onset on study day 13, identified by the investigator as “mild” where outcome was “recovered/resolved” after discontinuation. There were two events experienced from a single patient where the adverse event entries were “nystagmus” and “diplopia”. The event of “diplopia” occurred on study day 29, was judged by the investigator as “moderate” intensity with an outcome variable entry (AEOUT) as “not recovered/resolved”. The narrative report of the “diplopia” event on study day 29 does not indicate continuation of the event, although the AEOUT shows “not recovered/not resolved” The second event entered for this patient was “nystagmus” where the event was judged as “moderate” intensity” with onset on study day 48 and identified as “not recovered/resolved” in the AEOUT (outcome variable). The narrative indicates the outcome entry was derived from examination 2 hours after drug discontinuation. The final “diplopia” event is entered on study day 71 as “mild” with an AEOUT entry of “recovered/resolved”.

L713: ## *Hypophosphatemia*
L714: C013-C017 OLE Has similar frequency of post baseline OORR low (&lt; 2.5\text{meq/L}) values that are a decline from baseline, 20.4% (n=103), to that seen in C021. When ULN is set at 4.5\text{meq/L} then 17% of patients in the open label extension pool are OORR high. This is a more even distribution of low and high OORR compared to study C021.
L715: ## *Seizure Worsening Analysis*
L716: Examination of study C013 reveals there were 4 (3.7%) epilepsy preferred terms13 in the placebo group and 2 (1.8%) in the 200mg cenobamate treatment arm. In addition, three of these events were status epilepticus where 2 occurred in the placebo arm and 1 in the cenobamate arm. Examination of study C017 reveals there were 3 (2.8%) epilepsy events in the placebo group, 4 (3.7%) in the 100mg arm, 2 (1.8%) in the 200mg arm and 1 (0.9%) in the 400mg arm.
L717: In study C021 there were 43 (3.2%) patients that had an epilepsy related preferred term entry. Examination of the latency from cenobamate start to the study day of each epilepsy adverse event occurrence reveals tendency toward a steady distribution over time, see Figure 41. The absence of an early cluster of seizure related adverse events is an argument against cenobamate induced worsening of the underlying epilepsy.
L718: 13 Reviewer Selection of epilepsy – seizure related preferred terms: Seizure, Epilepsy, Seizure cluster, Partial seizures with secondary generalization, Simple partial seizures, Atonic seizures, Autonomic seizures, Partial seizures.
L719: **Figure 41 C021, Epilepsy Related Preferred Terms, Kaplan-Meier Plot of Events Remaining by Study Day**
L720: Reviewer Comment: The distribution of epilepsy related adverse event terms in the controlled trial data reveals a trend of decline in frequency with increase in cenobamate dose. In a seizure worsening there is an expectation that frequency would increase with increasing cenobamate dose. In the examination of study C021 there was a steady occurrence over time of seizure related adverse event terms that is more consistent with breakthrough seizures seen in a refractory population then in a situation where a treatment is producing seizure worsening. If a treatment precipitated seizure worsening it is likely to be an event with a temporal relationship to the start of new treatment. This property would likely produce a cluster of events early in the course of cenobamate therapy. This profile was not observed.

L727: ## SOC
L728: Examination of all TEAE entries in the DB pivotal study pool reveals the most frequently populated SOC is “Nervous System Disorders” in all dose groups. There is a dose related increase in frequency of events in this SOC with a range from 39% in the Placebo treatment arm to 72% in the 400mg arm. Second in frequency of events in SOCs is “general disorders and administrative site conditions where there is a dose relationship across cenobamate treatment groups. The SOC with the third most frequency proportion of adverse effects is “gastrointestinal disorders” where again there is a dose relationship, see Figure 42. The frequency of individual preferred terms contained within the SOCs will be discussed next.

L730: ## Preferred Terms
L731: Examination of TEAE in the cenobamate treatment arms of the double blind pivotal study pool reveals the most frequent adverse event is "somnolence" with a dose relationship and a maximum frequency of 37% in the 400mg treatment arm. The term "dizziness" follows, again with a dose response relationship and a maximum frequency of 33% in the 400mg treatment arm. Both of these terms are found in the SOC "nervous system disorders". The 3rd most frequent adverse event is "fatigue" with a dose response relationship and a maximum frequency of 24% in the 400mg treatment arm. This adverse event is in the "general disorders and administration site conditions" SOC. The 4th most frequent adverse event "Headache" is again in the "nervous system disorders" SOC but is without a dose response relationship. The maximum frequency of 12% is seen in the 200mg cenobamate treatment arm. "Diplopia" follows in 5th position of frequency, also in the "nervous system disorders" SOC. This adverse event has a dose response relationship with a maximum frequency of 15% in the 400mg treatment arm. In the 6th position of frequency is "nausea" from the "gastrointestinal disorders" SOC, without a dose response relationship but a maximum frequency of 9% in the 400mg treatment arm. The 7th position of frequency is "balance disorder" from the "nervous system disorders" SOC. This adverse event has a dose response relationship across cenobamate treatment. The adverse event "nystagmus" from the "nervous system disorders" SOC is the 8th most frequency adverse event associated with cenobamate treatment but does not have a dose response. The maximum frequency is 6.7% seen in the 200mg treatment arm. In the 9th position of frequency in the cenobamate treatment arms is "upper respiratory tract infection" from the "infections and infestations" SOC, without a dose response relationship but with maximum frequency seen in the 200mg treatment arm. The 10th most frequent adverse event is "constipation" from the "gastrointestinal disorders" SOC. This adverse event has a dose response relationship with the maximum frequency of 8.1% in the 400mg treatment arm. The adverse event "Vomiting" also from the "gastrointestinal disorders" SOC, follows constipation in the 11th position of frequency with a dose response. The maximum frequency of 5.4% is seen in the 400mg treatment arm. The 12th position of adverse event frequency is "nasopharyngitis" from the SOC "infections and infestations" with a dose response relationship where the maximum frequency is 4.5%. The adverse event that is 13 in order of frequency is "gait disturbance" from the "General disorders and administration site conditions" SOC. This adverse event has a strong dose response relationship where the maximum frequency is 8.1% in the 400mg arm. In 14th position of frequency is the adverse event "ataxia" from the SOC "nervous system disorders" with a dose response relationship and a maximum frequency of 6.3% in the 400mg treatment arm. In 15th position of frequency is the adverse event "back pain" from the SOC "musculoskeletal and connective tissue disorders". There is no dose response relationship and a maximum frequency of 5.4% is seen in the 400mg arm. The 16th position of adverse event frequency is observed for the term "urinary tract infection", from the SOC "infections and infestations". There is no dose response and a maximum frequency of 5.4% is seen in the 200mg arm. In 17th position of frequency is the term "diarrhoea" from the SOC "gastrointestinal disorders" with a dose response relationship and a maximum frequency of 4.5% in the 400mg dose arm. The next (18th) most frequent adverse event is "dysarthria" from the SOC "nervous system disorders" without a
L732: dose response relationship and a maximum frequency of 7.2% seen in the 400mg treatment arm. The 19th most frequent adverse event is “fall” from the “injury, poisoning and procedural complications” SOC. This adverse event has a dose response with a maximum frequency of 3.6% in the 400mg dose group. The 20th most frequent adverse event is “vision blurred” from the “eye disorders” SOC with a dose response relationship and a maximum frequency of 3.6% in the 400mg dose arm. In 21st place from the top adverse event frequency is “decreased appetite” from the “gastrointestinal disorders” SOC without a dose response relationship and a maximum frequency of 5.4% seen in the 400mg treatment arm. The last adverse event to be covered, in position of 22nd most frequent is “vertigo” from the “ear and labyrinth disorders” SOC. This adverse event has a dose response relationship with a maximum frequency of 6.3% seen in the 400mg treatment arm. This assessment covers all adverse event where there were greater than 10 events counted among all three cenobamate treatment arms, see Table 41.
L733: From among the 22 adverse events in the prior discussion by frequency in the cenobamate treatment arms all but “upper respiratory tract infection”, “back pain”, “urinary tract infection” and “decreased appetite” had a dose response relationship. From among those remaining, with a dose response relationship, there were 5 adverse events where the placebo treatment arm had a frequency greater than one of the cenobamate arms. The placebo arm had a higher frequency than the low dose cenobamate arm for the adverse event terms “nasopharyngitis”, “gait disturbance”, and “vertigo” while the low dose (100mg) cenobamate arm was equal to the frequency in placebo for the term “ataxia”. The frequency of the adverse event “fall” was higher in the placebo arm than in any cenobamate treatment arm, see Table 41.

L737: Reviewer Comment: The TEAE with a high frequency, a dose response and a frequency greater than placebo carry the greatest evidence for a causal relationship to the study drug. The top 22 adverse events in order of overall count among the cenobamate treatment arms were surveyed to assess this relationship. The majority of these events are related to CNS or gastrointestinal dysfunction and include “somnolence”, “dizziness”, “fatigue”, “headache”, “diplopia”, “nausea”, “balance disorder”, “nystagmus”, “constipation”, “vomiting”, “diarrhea”, “dysarthria”, and “vision blurred”. The events related to CNS dysfunction are more frequent early in cenobamate exposure. The majority of these adverse events occur in the first 40 days of treatment (Figure 43) and will be noted in the label section 5. These effect may be mitigated in part by the reduced dose escalation in the prescribing instructions compared to the titration rate in the pivotal studies. The adverse event terms from the SOC “gastrointestinal disorders” have a similar distribution where they are most prominent in the first 40 days of cenobamate treatment, see Figure 44

L767: There were 48 entries from 35 (6.9%) patients for the term seizure. There were no cenobamate discontinuations entered for these events although there are 4 entries for “dose reduced” and 3 for “not applicable” entered. These events do not appear related to lack of efficacy associated with early occurrence or loss of efficacy, related to duration of treatment, see the Kaplan–Meier plot for occurrence of event by study day. The maximum exposure in days by patient is also shown for comparison, see Figure 47.

L769: ## Psychiatric Terms &gt;1%
L770: There were 57 entries from 46 (9.1%) patients with the preferred terms “anxiety (3%)”, “depression (1.6%)”, “irritability (2.6%)”, and “suicidal ideation (1.8%)”. None of these terms was entered as an SAE and cenobamate was withdrawn from 4 patients. three of the four withdrawals were for “depression” and the remaining for “anxiety”. Approximately half of these adverse events occur in the first year with 80% occurring in approximately the first 2 years of treatment, see Figure 48. The adverse events of suicidal ideation have a predominance of occurrence in the first year of treatment where 7 of 9 events occurred between day 169 and 396.
L771: **Figure 48 Study C013-C017, TEAE, Kaplan–Meier plot, Proportion AE Remaining by Study day: Psychiatric Preferred Terms “anxiety”, “depression”, “irritability” and “suicidal ideation”.**
L772: ## Gastrointestinal Disorders Terms
L773: The terms “constipation”, “abdominal pain” and “gastroenteritis” are examined as possibly related to the high frequency of the adverse event “appendicitis”. These terms may reflect change in gastrointestinal motility or tendency for infection. There were 62 entries for these terms from 51 patients. There was one cenobamate withdrawal for an event of “constipation” and one entry as an SAE for the term “gastroenteritis”. Thirty for entries were judged by the investigator as “mild” and 24 as “moderate” by the investigator. Four were judged as “severe”. These were 3 entries for “abdominal pain” and one entry of “constipation”. *Constipation is present in proposed labeling in the adverse event table.*
L774: ## Potential Hypersensitivity Events
L775: Potential hypersensitivity events are captured by examination of the “immune system disorders” and “Skin and subcutaneous tissue disorders” SOCs. In these two categories there were 116 entries from 76 (15%) patients. In this group there were entries from 42 preferred terms where 15 terms with 51 entries from 40 (8%) patients were selected as potentially related to hypersensitivity. Terms were recoded to consolidate similar events. All dermatitis terms were recoded to “dermatitis” and all rash terms were recoded to “rash” see Table 44. From among these terms none was entered as an SAE. There were 3 cenobamate withdrawals,
L776: one each for “angioedema”, “pruritus”, and “rash”. The “angioedema” and “rash” events were judged as “mild” severity and the “pruritus” was judged as “moderate” severity. **Assessment:** *There were 8% of patients overall with these terms but no SAEs for potential hypersensitivity in this OLE safety dataset. The term “angioedema” is of concern to possible relationship to DRESS but this was judged as mild but did result in drug withdraw. These findings are consistent with the hypersensitivity signal that has been identified.*

L779: Reviewer Comment: TEAE seen in C013-C017 Pooled OLE reveal adverse event frequencies that are in similar to those identified in the double-blind pivotal studies. Terms related to CNS dysfunction are most common and have a predominant occurrence within the first six months of cenobamate treatment. The adverse events in the psychiatric disorder SOC are consistent with the class effect of AEDs and do not appear to demonstrate a notable departure from the existing signal. The “seizure” adverse events are not an independent safety signal. These events are not a notable departure from the background occurrence of seizure in a refractory epilepsy population. The gastrointestinal events of “constipation”, “abdominal pain” and “gastroenteritis” are not of frequency and intensity to indicate a novel safety signal in cenobamate treatment. The hypersensitivity associated events are in alignment with a tendency for activation of hypersensitivity.

L782: in the safety dataset (positive safety flag 1399 patients15).

The duration of exposure and modal dose distribution may be seen in section 8.2.1, C021 Modal Dose.

L807: ## Select TEAE that have a frequency 1%≥ are examined:
L808: With the following methodology; the preferred terms are examined for content of special interest based on the potential relationship to a serious medical condition that if related to study drug treatment may be of special concern. In addition, the preferred terms are examined for potential relationship to submission specific concerns of hypersensitivity and short QT – cardiac related events. Preferred terms from this group that are entered as SAEs will also be captured and selectively examined. Examination of the subset of preferred terms with a frequency ≤1% yields 60 terms from 219 patients with a total of 234 adverse event instances. This examination captured 28 preferred term entries with only one instance.
L809: From among this subset of the C021 TEAE adverse events the most frequent was “alanine aminotransferase increased” (1%), with no SAEs, “eosinophilia” occurred in 12 patients (1%) with no SAEs or associated cenobamate discontinuation. Eleven patients experienced “dyspnea” occurred in 11 patients with no SAEs and 1 drug discontinuation, see above discussion in study C021 “preferred terms of interest”.
L810: There were 10 patients who experienced pneumonia where 5 occurred as an SAE (1 patient, YKP3089C021-(b)(6) had 2 (SAE) episode of pneumonia). The first event was judged as mild by the investigator on study day 56. A second event judged as “moderate” severity occurred on study day 74. No clear etiology is identified, cenobamate was continued until day 713. Examination of clinical laboratory studies does not reveal evidence of myelosuppression. A second SAE entry for pneumonia occurred on study day 4. This is early for a physiologic relationship to cenobamate. The investigator judged the event as “mild” intensity and cenobamate was continued until study day 92. The patient maximum dose began on study day 71 as 200mg. The third patient with an SAE of pneumonia had this adverse event on study day 161, entered as “severe” intensity but cenobamate was continued. No underlying laboratory abnormalities were identified that may be associated with pneumonia such as evidence of myelosuppression. The patient continued in the study until day 379 and had a maximum cenobamate dose of 400mg. There were 2 additional cases of pneumonia as an SAE. These occurred later on the treatment timeline reducing causality based on temporal relationship. The times to onset in one of the two cases was at 1 year and in the second of the two at 14 months. Cenobamate was continued. In the remaining 7 cases the events were judged as “mild” or “moderate” intensity and cenobamate was continued unchanged in all. The time to onset in these remaining cases ranged from 76 days to 694 days.
L811: There were 8 patients who experience an adverse event of “rash papular” where in 3 cases the
L812: event was an SAE. In two of these three cases cenobamate was discontinued the time to onset in the three cases was 41 days, 139 days and 317 days. The case that had TTO of 41 days there was a skin biopsy performed that revealed “spongiotic reaction with dermal perivascular infiltrate and eosinophilia, consistent with a drug reaction”. The cenobamate dose at time of onset was 50mg a day. Escalation from 25mg to 50mg had occurred on study day 28. The remaining two “rash papular” cases entered as an SAE were judged as “mild” intensity by the investigator and will be called case 1 and case 2. Case 1 occurred on study day 139 while the patient was on 200mg of cenobamate, the patient reported feeling itchy and hot. The investigator judged the event as “mild” severity and the event resolved in 5 days after cenobamate discontinuation. Case 2 occurred on study day 317 while the patient was taking 400mg of cenobamate. The patient had a bilateral flank papular eruption progressing to the lower limbs, there was no other evidence of systemic hypersensitivity. The event resolved 12 days after discontinuation of cenobamate.
L813: There were 7 patients who experienced a TEAE of “erythema”, one entered as an SAE. The event resolved approximately 1 month after drug discontinuation. The remaining six TEAEs of erythema were judged as “mild” severity by the investigator and cenobamate was continued.
L814: There were 7 patients who experienced “hyperkalemia” where none was an SAE but cenobamate was discontinued in one case where there was an associated elevation of ALT and AST. Potassium was found to be elevated to 6.3meq/L on study day 60 at a cenobamate dose of 100mg, with an associated elevation of ALT to 16 x ULN and AST to 2.8 x ULN. The narrative indicates last dose of cenobamate on study day 65 with normal potassium and AST values obtained at an unscheduled visit on study day 66 and ALT improved to a level of 2.7 x ULN. The remaining six patients with an event of “hypokalemia” had severity judged as “mild” by the investigator and cenobamate continued in all cases. The median time to the “hypokalemia” events was 77 days.

L822: with only a single occurrence (0.07%). There were 10 events in this category. Eight of these events were captured as an SAE in study C021 in section 8.4.2 or Section 8.4.1 Deaths. There were two events that were SAEs, not previously captured. The first was an SAE of “serositis” experienced by patient YKP3089C021-(b) (6) on study day 140, on a cenobamate dose of 150mg. The narrative report indicates there was a prodrome of fatigue and not feeling well, possibly dating to the initiation of cenobamate. The patient presented to the emergency room with fever and pleuritic chest pain and was found to have a pericardial rub. A pleural and pericardial effusion were identified on imaging. CRP was highly elevated at 283mg/L (reference range &lt;3mg/l) and hemoglobin 8.7g/dL. Cenobamate was discontinued. The investigator indicates this adverse event may be confounded by *concomitant treatment with dasatinib*, (SPRYCEL) where pleural and pericardial effusions have been seen in clinical trials. The patient improved at the next assessment 9 days after cenobamate was discontinued with decline of CRP to 84mg/L and Hb increased to 10.9g/dL. The next event captured in the low frequency TEAE assessment where events were also entered as SAEs was a case of “myelopathy”. This event was related to a seizure related injury and fall.

L837: An assessment of the median time to TEAE preferred term occurrence in study C021 and the associated frequency of the preferred terms is shown in Figure 49 and Figure 50. This purpose of this assessment is to identify the temporal relationship between start of cenobamate treatment and onset of the adverse effect. The basis of interpretation is that a shorter temporal relationship (tighter coupling between onset and event) is supportive of causality compared to events that occur sporadically scattered across the study timeline. This assessment was partially informative. Several events of unexpected causal relationship occurred in close relationship to start of treatment. These events were “neuropathy peripheral”, “skin discolouration”. These are adverse reactions that characteristically have a duration to manifestation longer than 10 days. Adverse events that occurred where a close temporal relationship increases plausible causality were “pharyngeal erythema” and “skin burning sensation”, that may be a hypersensitivity phenomenon. Adverse events seen to have a median temporal relationship with a maximum of 80 days (Figure 49), where causal association is plausibly increased by this relationship, were “abasia”, “rash maculo-papular”, “rash erythematous”, “dermatitis atopic”, “aspartate aminotransferase increased”, “aggression”, “rash papular”, “agitation”, and “alanine aminotransferase increased”. These are events associated with the hypersensitivity potential of cenobamate or the central nervous system dysfunction seen with cenobamate. The corresponding frequency of these events is shown in Figure 50.

L839: **Figure 50 Study C021, Instances of TEAE by Preferred Terms, TEAE Frequency Group \le 1\%, SAE and Discontinuation Excluded**
L840: ## Reviewer Comment, C021 TEAE:
L841: Study C021 has the greatest number of patient years of exposure but not the longest duration of exposure, this is due to a larger patient population in study C021. Longest duration is captured in the open label extensions of Studies C013 and C021. The spectrum of TEAEs seen in this open label exposure are very similar to those seen in the C013-C017 open label extension.
L842: The highest frequency events, seen in &gt;10% of patients, were related to CNS dysfunction, the preferred terms include “somnolence”, “dizziness”, and “fatigue”. Headache, a fourth term with an occurrence &gt;10% is not a CNS dysfunction term. The basis for “headache” is uncertain. Additional frequent events include “Viral upper respiratory tract infection”, “Nausea”, “Upper respiratory tract infection”, “diplopia”, “balance disorder”, “constipation”, “vision blurred”, “diarrhoea”, and “fall” at frequencies of 8%, 7%, 7%, 6%, 6% 4%, 4%, 4%, and 4% respectively. Again, the majority of these terms are related to CNS dysfunction. The events of “Viral upper respiratory tract infection”, “Nausea”, “Upper respiratory tract infection”, are not unexpected events due to a high background rate. “Constipation” occurs at approximately the same rate as “diarrhoea” thus it is possible these events are influenced by background rate. “nausea”, “vomiting” and “diarrhoea” were judged generally as “mild” or “moderate” intensity, therefore do not appear to represent a safety threat.
L843: “Pneumonia” occurs in 10 patients where 5 have an entry as an SAE. Judgement on the causality and safety threat of this event is difficult due to a high background rate of pneumonia. The events did not have a consistent temporal relationship to start of cenobamate treatment and in 7 of 10 cases were judged as “mild” or “moderate” severity. This event does not rise to threshold for entry into labeling.
L844: Low frequencies of electrolyte abnormalities occur including hypokalemia and hyponatremia, but these are likely sporadic background events. There are 4 events of deep vein thrombosis”, in one case related to a severe cardiac event but otherwise appear sporadic and not coupled to cenobamate treatment.
L845: The low frequency but highly serious cardiac adverse events are captured in the discussion of “serious adverse events” and “discontinuations”
L846: Hypersensitivity events of rash, dermatitis, drug eruption, and “eosinophil count increased” are consistent with the hypersensitivity signal that has been identified in association with cenobamate treatment.

L857: Reviewer Comment: Analysis of the mean change from baseline and group mean value by study visit for select laboratory parameters was performed. The mean changes from baseline reveal a small mean increase in alkaline phosphatase at all visit that is reflected in the summary mean of the cenobamate treatment arm. There is no clear mechanism seen during the analysis of adverse events. Examination for OORR high values, next section, does not reveal a notable difference in distribution from placebo. Chloride reveals a decline from baseline compared to placebo at visits 6 and 12. There is also a decline in the overall mean for all visits in cenobamate treatment compared to placebo. Glucose had a positive change from baseline at visits 4, 6, and 8 in the cenobamate treatment arm compared to placebo. Examination of the group mean values by visit also reveals a small increase over placebo at all but visit 7. There is a consistent trend of positive mean change from baseline for potassium at all visits compared to placebo. The frequency of OORR high values for potassium is examined in the next section (outlier values). Examination of serum sodium reveals a small positive change from baseline in the cenobamate treatment arm not present in the placebo arm at visits 8, 10 and 12. Examination of the Erythrocyte Distribution Width (RDW) mean change from baseline reveals the cenobamate treatment arm has a positive value at all visits and an increase over the placebo change from baseline at all visits. The distribution of RDW values and OORR high outliers is presented in the next section. There is a negative change from baseline in the cenobamate treatment arm for hemoglobin values at all study visits that is larger than seen in the placebo arm. There is also a negative change in group mean values from baseline at all study visits for leukocyte measurements. This is also greater than the change from baseline for placebo at corresponding visit. This is explored further in the next section. Like leukocytes the neutrophil values had a negative group mean change from baseline at all study visits post baseline, also greater in magnitude than any values in the placebo arm. This same trend is seen in for platelets.
L858: In summary, there is a mild myelosuppressive effect seen in the cenobamate treatment arm compared to placebo. The mean changes from baseline are not of large magnitude but suggest a possible consistent systematic effect and raises the question whether this change is related to the shift to a higher RDW value in the cenobamate treatment arm. This will be explored further in the examination of hematologic outliers in the next section. There is a small change toward increase in sodium and potassium values.

Both patients were in the cenobamate treatment arm.
L876: ## Platelets
L877: There were no placebo patients with a shift from normal baseline to OORR high post baseline while there was one cenobamate treatment patient with a shift from 1.009 \times ULN at baseline to 1.22 \times ULN at maximum post baseline value.

L879: ## Leukocytes
L880: There were two each placebo arm and cenobamate treatment arm patients where the post baseline maximum value was a shift from normal to OORR high. The maximum increase was seen in a cenobamate treatment patient with an upshift of 5.9 \times 10^9 /L.
L881: There were 20 patients in the cenobamate treatment arm and 15 in the placebo arm with a minimum (maximal decline) value that was a shift to OORR low at any post baseline visit. The mean decline of the 15 placebo patients was -0.32 while the mean decline in the cenobamate patients was -0.41 with a median of -0.27 and -0.35 respectively.

L898: ## **Reviewer Comment, C013 Laboratory Summary:**
L899: Calculation of group mean change from baseline by study (Table 47) visit for the selected laboratory parameters reveals a small mean negative change from baseline for hemoglobin, neutrophils, platelets, leukocytes, and chloride. Where there is a similar change in the placebo arm the change in the cenobamate arm is larger. There is a small mean increase in the cenobamate treatment are seen for RDW, alkaline phosphatase, glucose, potassium and sodium.
L900: Group mean values (Table 48) reflect the calculated group mean changes except for chloride and sodium where there was no difference at any visit for mean values.
L901: Outlier values for the selected laboratory parameters are examined using JMP Clinical waterfall plots for both high and low outliers by observed result, greater than upper or lower limits of normal.
L902: The examination for OORR high values reveals notable abnormalities for eosinophils where there were 4 patients with an entry &gt; 2 \times \text{ULN} in the cenobamate treatment arm. Examination of corresponding adverse event dataset did not identify any entries for hypersensitivity reactions among these patients. Analysis of potassium values revealed 1 patient with a post baseline entry of 1.2 \times \text{ULN}. A subsequent change from baseline analysis revealed a &gt;1 \text{meq/L} increase in 6 cenobamate treated patients and 3 placebo treated patients. Examination of glucose revealed 12 patients in the cenobamate treatment arm with a &gt;36 \text{meq/L} increase and 10 patients in the placebo arm with an increase of the same magnitude.
L903: The low value examination reveals generally sporadic OORR low outliers. There were notable neutrophil outliers where 2 patients had entries for critical values that were less than 500 \times 10^9/\text{L}. These were seen in 2 patients. Examination of the adverse event profile reveals that one of these patients has a viral infection during baseline that may account for the neutropenia. The second patient had no confounding event but was a sporadic value rather than part of a sequential trend. Neither patient had a post baseline infection. There was a single patient with a platelet value that decline to 64 \times 10^9/\text{L}. This value was a sporadic lowering of a profile of values that were all OORR low at all entries including baseline. Leukocyte evaluation revealed two patients with values of 2.7 \times 10^9/\text{L}. One of these patients had a low baseline value while the second had a low normal value at baseline with a single sporadic OORR low on study day 37.
L904: Shift examinations were performed on select hematology and chemistry parameters including hemoglobin, leukocytes, neutrophils, platelets, sodium, potassium, and glucose.
L905: The shift examinations revealed no substantive frequency or magnitude of change among the minimum or maximum, high or low values that were OORR during the post baseline treatment
L906: interval for the selected chemistry parameters. The shift examination also no substantive frequency or magnitude of change among the minimum or maximum, high or low values that were OORR during the post baseline treatment interval for the selected hematology parameters.
L907: Overall examination of the study C013 safety laboratory dataset reveals small mean positive or negative change from baseline across study visits that did no translate into substantive frequency of treatment related outliers or shifts from normal at baseline.

L914: Reviewer Comment: There is no clinically identifiable impact of the post baseline positive change in RDW.
L915: **Hemoglobin:** There is no notable group mean change from baseline visit or from placebo. Post baseline values were examined. There were 3 (3%), 1 (1%), 4 (3.6%), and 7 (6%) patients in the placebo, 100mg, 200mg and 400mg treatment arms with a decline from baseline &gt;1.5g/dL hemoglobin. Examination of the patient cohort with normal baseline hemoglobin values revealed that 1.9%, 6.5%, 2.7% and 2.7% in the placebo, 100mg, 200mg and 400mg treatment arm had OORR low post baseline hemoglobin values.
L916: Reviewer Comment: Based on the low frequency of OORR low values, sustained sequential decline and sporadic occurrence and small separation from placebo in the high dose treatment arms, there is no clinically substantive change in hemoglobin values related to cenobamate treatment are identified.
L917: ## **Leukocytes:**
L918: Examination of group mean leukocyte values by visit and treatment arm reveal a small decline in group mean values that has a duration and dose relationship in the 200mg and 400mg treatment arms. Examination for low outliers among patients with normal baseline reveals there was an increase frequency of patients with post baseline OORR low values, compared to placebo, in the 200mg and 400mg treatment arms. The highest frequency was in the 400mg arm. There was no dose related decline in leukocyte count among the patients with OORR low post baseline values, see *Table 52*. The percent change from baseline was examined. The overall trend of post baseline leukocyte values was assessed by examination of the proportion of patients that had a negative change compared to those with a positive change from baseline by treatment arm. There were a higher proportion of cenobamate treated patients with a negative percent change from baseline compared to the placebo treatment arm. This trend was also dose related where 39% of patients in the placebo arm, 53% of patients in the 200mg arm and 68% of patients in the 400mg cenobamate arm had a negative change in leukocyte value from baseline to double blind treatment interval, see *Table 53*. Among those patients with OORR low values one was observed with a value &lt;3.0 \times 10^9/L. This patient had a value of 2.7 \times 10^9/L.
L919: Reviewer Comment: there was a trend to post baseline decline in leukocyte count that had partial relationship to dose. Examination of the magnitude of the decline did not identify a trajectory to critically low values and in most cases the declines were not part of a sequential decline of leukocyte count. Labeling should reflect that a dose related frequency of low leukocyte counts was seen. This may be placed in laboratory abnormalities subsection of section 6.

L941: ## Hematology Summary
L942: Examination of the hematology parameters eosinophils, hemoglobin, erythrocyte distribution width, leukocytes, lymphocytes, neutrophils, and platelets reveals that a dose related decline in leukocytes to OORR low was present and there was an overall excess of post baseline decline in leukocyte count over an increase in leukocyte count. There was also a trend of decline in neutrophil count related to dose where the 400mg treatment arm had a notable separation from the frequency of OORR low post baseline values in the placebo treatment arm. There was also a higher frequency of post baseline declines in neutrophil count compared to post baseline increase in neutrophil count. The lowest values identified did not reach critical levels of 500 \times 10^9/L. The lowest values identified were 1.0 \times 10^9/L with two occurrences each in the 400mg treatment arm and the placebo arm. There were no notable changes in the remaining parameters that were examined. Section 6 of labeling in "laboratory abnormalities" should reflect that a decline in leukocytes to OORR low during cenobamate treatment was observed that was dose related where 3.7%, 3.7%, 6.4% and 9.9% of the placebo, 100mg, 200mg, and
L943: 400mg treatment arms respectively. The decline in neutrophils should also be included in the “laboratory abnormalities” in section 6 noting that the frequency of decline during cenobamate treatment to OORR low was 3% in the placebo arm and 8% in the 400mg arm.

L947: **ALT:** Examination of group mean ALT values by study visit and treatment arm reveals a small increase over placebo that is dose related. There is no consistent trend by study visit. Examination of OORR high outlier values reveals 5 patients with post baseline elevations &gt; 2 \times ULN. Four of these patients had a value &gt; 3 \times ULN and one had a value &gt; 2 \times ULN but &lt; 3 \times ULN. The patients that exceeded a value of 3 \times ULN have their values and associated value trend expressed as narrative in the following paragraph:
L948: YKP3089C017- [REDACTED](b)(6): sporadic V6 YKP3089C017- [REDACTED](b)(6): sporadic V9 YKP3089C017- [REDACTED](b)(6): peak of 7.6 \times ULN at V6 followed by steady decline to 2 \times ULN at V10 with no significant elevation of bilirubin. YKP3089C017- [REDACTED](b)(6): sporadic 3 \times ULN V6, 1.4 \times ULN @ V9. There was one additional patient with a post baseline value &gt; 2 \times ULN (with normal baseline). There were no other patients with values that exceeded 2 \times ULN with normal baseline.
L949: The mean value of both post baseline values is computed and compared to the baseline value. Examination of the distribution of the post baseline mean compared to the baseline reveals there was one post baseline mean that was in excess of 3 \times ULN, this was patient YKP3089C017- [REDACTED](b)(6) whose post baseline mean was 4.63 \times ULN. Analysis by this method revealed no mean of post baseline ALT values &gt; 2.4 \times ULN. There were 16 patients in the total of cenobamate treatment arms with a post baseline ALT value that was &gt; 100\% of baseline while there were none in the placebo treatment arm.
L950: Reviewer Comment: there is an elevation of ALT associated with cenobamate treatment found in group means and by percent change from baseline analyses. The effect was dose related and reached a critical threshold of &gt; 3 \times ULN in one case. The overall trend does not show a trajectory to critical elevation and examination of high outliers reveals the excursion to high OORR was sporadic. These observations do not reach a threshold of clinical concern for drug induced liver injury but is captured as an adverse event in labeling of elevated AST in the adverse event category of investigations.
L951: **AST:** Examination of group mean values by treatment arm and study visit reveals an elevation of AST that is in part dose related. Examination of outliers reveals 2 patients with a post baseline elevation of AST to &gt; 3 \times ULN. Both elevations are sporadic and not sustained in post baseline measurements.
L952: Reviewer Conclusion: There is no signal for clinically substantive elevation of AST.
L953: **Calcium:** examination of group mean calcium values across treatment arms reveals a small mean decline compared to baseline and placebo in the 200mg and 400mg treatment arms. Post treatment OORR High or Low calcium values were uncommon. There were 2 patients with small excursions to OORR low, one each in the 100mg and 200mg treatment arms. A systematic examination of post baseline OORR low values in patients with normal baseline reveals a small excess of OORR low values in the 400mg arm compared to placebo where three patients (2.7%) have OORR low values, Table 60. These values are examined and reveal there is
L954: a single OORR low occurrence in one patient “sporadic”. There are two post baseline low values in another patient that plateau and stop decline. There is a third patient with two post baseline OORR low values that reach a minimum then improve, a U-shaped curve, although not to within reference range.
L955: Reviewer conclusion: the frequencies of departure from reference range calcium values during cenobamate treatment does not reach a threshold of a clinically substantive effect.

L962: ## Potassium:
L963: Examination of group mean change from baseline reveals a small increase from placebo to cenobamate treatment without a dose response. Capture of maximum (nadir) OORR low post baseline values from all dose groups and double-blind treatment period visits reveals the placebo arm had a higher frequency of OORR low potassium value than any cenobamate treatment arm, *Table 62*. Analysis of the mean post baseline potassium values to identify patients with a post baseline trend of elevated potassium, reveals there were 2 (1.9%), 7 (6.5%), 7 (6.4%), and 5 (4.5%) patients in the placebo, 100mg, 200mg and 400mg treatment arms that had a mean value OORR high respectively. The associated group mean and median potassium values in the treatment arms were 4% over ULN, there was no dose response, in this analysis of post baseline means, *Table 63*. Capture of maximum (peak) OORR high post baseline values from all dose groups and double-blind treatment period visits identifies 37 patients where the maximum potassium value was OORR high. The highest post baseline maximum value of 5.9meq/L occurred in the 400mg treatment arm. The distribution of OORR high maximum values is as follows; 6 (5.6%), 9 (8.3%), 10 (9.1%), and 12 (10.8%) patients in the placebo, 100mg 200mg and 400mg treatment arms respectively, *Table 64*. The group mean elevation over ULN was 8% in all treatment arms. There was no dose response for mean potassium value but there was a dose response for frequency of OORR high, *Table 64*. The profile of individual potassium values from those 37 patients with OORR high values are shown at visits 3, 6, and 9, *Figure 53*. This graphic covers 107 measurements from 37 post baseline OORR high patients. There are no excursions above the critical value of 6.0meq/L. There are 45 values &gt; 5meq/L. In 26 of these instances the occurrence of OORR high potassium value is sporadic while in 11 instances there is a sequential increase from visit 3 to visit 9 with 2, 4 and 5 patients in the 100mg, 200mg and 400mg arm. The dose response in frequency of OORR high and maximum frequency of sequential potassium trend within individual patients from the 200mg and 400mg support a cenobamate treatment effect.

L971: ## *Reviewer Conclusion, K+:*
L972: Based on the frequency, dose response, and separation from placebo of increased serum potassium there is an association of elevation of serum potassium values with cenobamate treatment. The magnitude of the elevation in study C017 did not reach a CTCAE grade 3, however, the sponsor reports 2 patients in the C013-C017 OL extension pool had values in this range. The clinical meaning of the observations of potassium is uncertain. There is a higher baseline rate of OORR high than OORR low potassium. In the C017 laboratory dataset 27 (6.3%) patients had a baseline potassium value &gt; 5.0meq/L while 2 (0.46%) had a baseline value &lt;3.5meq/L. Although there was an excess of high baseline potassium values, the post baseline distribution of OORR high values was dose related with an excess frequency as cenobamate dose increased. The laboratory abnormalities subsection of section 6 should reflect that OORR high potassium values were in excess of placebo in a dose related distribution, occurring in 5.6%, 8.3%, 9.1% and 10.8% of the placebo, 100mg, 200mg, and 400mg treatment arms respectively, with a maximum value of 5.9meq/L.
L973: **Glucose:** Examination of group mean glucose values across treatment arms and study visits does not reveal a consistent change. When the dataset is examined for a maximum value at any study visit that is OORR high from among patients with normal baseline values 6 patients are captured. There are 2 (1.9%), 0 (0%), 3 (2.7%), and 1 (0.9%) from the placebo, 100mg, 200mg and 400mg treatment arms respectively, *Table 65*. The mean post baseline glucose value is computed, and this mean value is compared to baseline to generate a percent change (mean) from baseline. The frequency of a positive percent change from baseline is greater than the frequency of a negative change from baseline in all treatment arms including placebo, *Table 66*. When the frequency of positive percent change from baseline is examined there is only a small increase in treatment arm frequency over placebo positive change frequency.

L981: ## Sodium
L982: Examination of group mean values by study visit and across treatment arms does not reveal any change in sodium values. Examination of outlier values during treatment reveal several significant OORR low values. There were 11 outliers with a serum sodium value &lt; 126 at visit
L983: There were 7 patients normal or low at baseline that continued to decline and had a post baseline sodium value of 125meq/L or less. The timeline of adverse events and concomitant medications for each of these 7 patients is examined, *Figure 54*. Patient YKP3089C017-(b)(6) had low sodium values at baseline and both treatment visits where the visit 6 value was 132meq/L and the Visit 9 value was 123 meq/L. There were no adverse events related clearly to hyponatremia. The patient was on concomitant oxcarbazepine. Patient YKP3089C017-(b)(6) had low sodium values at all three samples. Baseline value was 129meq/L, Visit 6 was 126meq/L and visit 9 was 125meq/L. The patient had CNS adverse effects coded a mild and was on concomitant oxcarbazepine. Patient YKP3089C017-(b)(6) had a low baseline value, visit 6 was in reference range of 140meq/L and Visit 9 was 121meq/L. The patient was also on ongoing oxcarbazepine. Patient YKP3089C017-(b)(6) had low baseline at 128meq/L with Visit 6 and visit 9 values of 121meq/L and 132meq/L respectively. The patient had CNS adverse effects and was on concomitant oxcarbazepine. Patient YKP3089C017-(b)(6) had all three sodium values OORR low, 126, 123, 125 meq/L at baseline, Visit 6 and Visit 9 respectively. The adverse events were rash, and fatigue and the patient was on concomitant oxcarbazepine. Patient YKP3089C017-(b)(6) had a within reference range baseline but visit 6 and visit 9 were 125 and 130meq/L respectively. The were adverse effects of “hyponatraemia” and “hypochloreaemia” as well as dizziness and fatigue. The patient was also on concomitant oxcarbazepine. Patient YKP3089C017-(b)(6) had sodium values of 127, 125 and 127meq/L at baseline, visit 6 and visit 9 respectively. The only adverse effect was fatigue and the patient was on concomitant oxcarbazepine. This majority of patients in this sample had low baseline sodium and all were on the confounding concomitant AED oxcarbazepine. Examination of this sample of seven patients with the lowest serum sodium values reveals a high frequency of central nervous system adverse effects but these are confounded by the background effect of cenobamate. The temporal profile of the low sodium values does not have a consistent pattern.
L984: Only one patient (YKP3089C017-[REDACTED](b)(6)) has a sequential stepwise decline from a low baseline. In the remainder the sequence is irregular, see *Figure 54*.

L989: Carbamazepine and oxcarbazepine are common concomitant AEDs that may cause hyponatremia. The proportion of patients in overall study treatment arms on carbamazepine or oxcarbazepine is shown in *Table 69*. This table reveals a small difference in exposure to confounding concomitant medication between the placebo and cenobamate treatment arms, where placebo exposure is 6% higher, *Table 69*. Independent of a cenobamate effect on depression of sodium values, this proportionality of exposure to oxcarbazepine and carbamazepine makes it more likely that placebo arm would have the highest frequency of hyponatremia. However, this is not true and the 200mg and 400mg treatment arm have the highest frequency of hyponatremia, *Table 68*.
L990: An analysis is performed to determine the proportion of C017 study patients on these medications and the overlap with patients who had OORR low sodium values. It was found that there were 35 patients in study C017 with an OORR low serum Na+ at post baseline scheduled visits (Visit 6 &amp; 9). Twenty of these 35 patients also had OORR low baseline values. Examination of the concomitant medication dataset reveals that when entries in the “ongoing” treatment variable CMENRF, or concomitant medication category variable CMCAT are positive, there were 26 patients on oxcarbazepine, 7 on carbamazepine and 2 on neither of these medications. From among these patients, a group of interest where post baseline values continued to decline from normal at baseline or low at baseline were selected. This subset consisted of 27 patients.
L991: Concomitant treatment with oxcarbazepine or carbamazepine was examined in the 27-patient subset that had a continued trajectory of post baseline decline. All but one patient in this group was treated with concomitant oxcarbazepine or carbamazepine. The single patient not on a concomitant that can lower serum sodium was in the placebo treatment arm, *Table 70*.

L998: ## Graph Builder
L999: ## *Reviewer Comment, Sodium:*
L1000: There is a high frequency of post baseline OORR low values found in patients with normal baseline values or had OORR low baseline values where decline continued. All but one of these occurrences is confounded by concomitant carbamazepine or more frequently oxcarbazepine. The single OORR post baseline low value that was not confounded by a concomitant AED that may lower serum sodium was a patient in the placebo arm. There were also several instances where the post baseline value meets CTCAE category 3 criteria.
L1001: The occurrence of post baseline OORR low values are more frequent in cenobamate treatment than in the placebo with the highest frequency in the 200mg and 400mg arms. Although all OORR low values in the treatment arms are confounded by concomitant medications, a clinically meaningful synergy between cenobamate and oxcarbazepine or carbamazepine may be possible. This is because the distribution of confounding AEDs across all treatment arms reveals the highest frequency of oxcarbazepine and carbamazepine concomitants is in the placebo arm. Labeling should have language that reflects these conditions of hyponatremia.
L1002: ## *Chemistry Summary:*
L1003: Examination of ALT values revealed one patient with a value of 7.6 x ULN that was sporadic and followed by a steady decline in values. Three were no elevations of bilirubin above the ULN. The observations on ALT values did not reach a threshold of clinical concern. There was a post baseline excess of serum potassium values that were OORR high in the cenobamate treatment arms over the placebo arm. There was maximal separation see between the placebo and 400mg treatment arm. There were 5.6% of patients in the placebo arm with post baseline OORR high values while in the 400mg treatment arm there were 10.8% of patients with post baseline OORR high values. This should be reflected in labeling as noted in the reviewer conclusion on potassium. There was an excess of post baseline OORR low sodium values in the cenobamate treatment arms. This excess is confounded by concomitant treatment with oxcarbazepine or carbamazepine in all cases. This observed excess is not in alignment with the overall distribution of oxcarbazepine and carbamazepine concomitant treatment in the overall study population where there was an excess of oxcarbazepine and carbamazepine treatment in the placebo treatment arm. Thus, an independent effect of cenobamate on the decline in serum sodium values appears to be present. This should be reflected in section 6 of labeling as identified in the reviewer comment on sodium (above).
L1004: ## 8.4.8. C013-C017 OLE (Sponsor Presentation) Open Label Extension Period
L1005: ## *Hematology*
L1006: Means
L1007: In the long term there was a decrease in platelets throughout the timepoints up to 36 months that did not reach a clinically meaningful level. Thereafter a small mean increase in platelet counts was noted.
L1008: ## Shifts
L1009: For the majority of hematology parameters shifts from normal baseline values to abnormal values were limited to 5 or fewer subjects. This was noted at any timepoint post baseline. No clinically meaningful trends were observed in shifts from baseline normal to abnormal values at post baseline timepoints.
L1010: ## Outliers
L1011: The majority of subjects' hematology parameters had a reported maximum post-baseline intensity of mild (CTCAE grade 1) for decreased lymphocytes and platelets. For decreased neutrophils, subjects had similar maximum post-baseline intensities of mild and moderate for both the cenobamate DB to OLE and placebo DB to cenobamate OLE groups. Subjects had a reported maximum post-baseline intensity of severe (CTCAE grade 3) for the following parameters:
L1012: - Decreased hemoglobin: 1/340 (0.3%) subject in the cenobamate DB to OLE group
- Decreased lymphocytes: 1/162 (0.6%) subject in the placebo DB to cenobamate OLE group

- Decreased platelets: 1/338 (0.3%) subject in the cenobamate DB to OLE group and 1/162 (0.6%) subject in the placebo DB to cenobamate OLE group

L1023: ## C021
L1024: ## Strategy
L1025: The strategy for review of study C021 hematology and clinical chemistry laboratory parameters will be to enter the sponsor overview analyses followed by a screening of core laboratory parameters using JMP clinical shift plot of mean post baseline values to screen for notable divergence in the cenobamate treatment interval. There will be a detailed exploration where a possible treatment effect has been noted in the placebo-controlled intervals of studies C013 and C017 as well as the phase 1 studies. These parameters will include hemoglobin, leukocytes, neutrophils, serum potassium and sodium.
L1026: ## Hematology
L1027: ## Sponsor Presentation (ISS p 126):
L1028: During Study YKP3089C021, there were no clinically meaningful trends in mean changes from baseline at each visit for any hematology variables. Percentage eosinophils, monocytes, lymphocytes, and erythrocyte distribution width percentage remained higher than baseline values for most of the treatment period for all safety evaluable groups at each visit. Erythrocyte counts, hematocrit, hemoglobin, leukocyte counts, lymphocyte counts, neutrophil counts and percentages, and platelet counts remained lower than baseline values for most of the treatment period for all safety evaluable groups. One AE each of anemia, eosinophilia, platelet count decreased (all from the Cenobamate and Other Concomitant AEDs group), and eosinophil count increased (from the Cenobamate and phenytoin group) led to study treatment discontinuation. None of these AEs were considered severe or serious. None of the AEs had an outcome of death.
L1029: By-visit shifts in percentage eosinophils, mean corpuscular volume, erythrocyte distribution width percentage, percentage lymphocytes, mean platelet volume, and percentage monocytes trended to higher from normal at baseline. By-visit shifts in mean corpuscular hemoglobin concentration, erythrocyte counts, leukocyte counts, and neutrophil counts trended to low from normal at baseline. In general, the number of shifts to either high or low values relative to the overall numbers of samples at any visit was very low. Although there were frequent shifts of the erythrocyte distribution with percentage to high from normal, the mean increases at any visit were low (1% to 3%), and mean levels at each visit were similar to the mean baseline levels.

L1060: ## Erythrocyte Distribution Width (RDW)
L1061: &gt;ULN: Visual inspection of the baseline to post baseline mean value plot reveals a trend toward post baseline mean &gt; baseline RDW value. All patients with a post baseline mean &gt; 1.2 x ULN (n=37) that was elevated over baseline mean were examined for distribution among the post baseline mean plots of eosinophils, lymphocytes, leukocytes, and neutrophils. The adverse event profile, serum ALT and globulin values were evaluated in this 37-patient subset. There was no pattern of adverse events or consistent relation to ALT values or serum among these patients.
L1062: Reviewer Comment: RDW is a measure of the variability in the size of circulating red blood cells (RBCs) and is a derived from automated hematology analyzers. RDW reflects the heterogeneity of erythrocyte volume and mean corpuscular volume (MCV) and is characterized as an electronic equivalent of erythrocyte anisocytosis as is registered light-microscopically in blood smears. RDW is a coefficient of variation of erythrocyte volume (in percentage, %), RDW expresses the dispersion of the RBC volume curve. There have been recent studies that show an association between inflammation, oxidative stress, negative outcome in vascular diseases and an increased RDW. The trend to post baseline increase in RDW seen during cenobamate treatment is of interest due to the pro-hypersensitive properties of cenobamate. It is reasonable to query if this pro-hypersensitive potential translates into a pro-inflammatory property. Exploration of the RDW trend in study C021 did not reveal substantive associations with inflammatory events.
L1063: ## *Hematology Reviewer Summary:*
L1064: There were an excess of post baseline declines in hemoglobin, lymphocytes, neutrophils, and leukocytes. The decline in all four parameters tended to plateau rather than follow a trajectory of continuing sequential decline. Associated adverse events were uncommon. Based on data from the pivotal studies labeling should include the decline seen in neutrophils and leukocytes. Red cell distribution width increased post baseline. There was no association with a change in eosinophils, lymphocytes, leukocytes, and neutrophils. The cause is unclear.

L1066: Sponsor Presentation (ISS p 132):

L1068: clearance, direct bilirubin, and sodium trended lower over the course of treatment. The remainder of the serum chemistry variables showed no change over time. And none of the AEs had an outcome of death. By-visit shifts in creatinine and globulin trended to low from normal at baseline and shifts in creatinine clearance trended to high from normal at baseline but in general, the number of shifts to either high or low values relative to the overall numbers of samples at any visit was very low.

Patient YKP3089C021-(b)(6) has persistent elevation of AST to OORR high during the study and concurrent elevations of ALT to OORR high but no elevation of bilirubin values. Based on the taper interval described in protocol YKP3089C021 Amendment 4, July 28, 2017 the last dose of cenobamate 100mg is projected to have occurred on study day 642. The AST and ALT elevation occurred 72 days after last dose of cenobamate. The relationship is uncertain and unlikely due to the absence of temporal relationship. Patient YKP3089C021-(b)(6) had a single OORR high elevation of 477 U/L on study day 42 with associated increases in ALT and ALP but no elevation of bilirubin. Patient YKP3089C021-(b)(6) had intermittent elevations of AST. There were associated elevations of ALT but no OORR high elevations of bilirubin. The maximum elevation of AST occurred on study day 274 that occurs 66 days after final cenobamate treatment where the preceding value on study day 211 (treatment end date is 207) was normal. Again, there is no temporal relationship to cenobamate treatment and the 4 day post treatment value was within reference range. Patient YKP3089C021-(b)(6) had
L1080: persistent elevation of AST during the course of the study (to study day 182) with multiple OORR high elevations of ALT and ALP also present. There is no OORR high bilirubin value. The past medical history reveals “cirrhosis alcoholic”. The persistent elevations are likely related to the underlying hepatic disease.

L1082: ## Reviewer Comment, ALT:
L1083: There were 308 (23.4%) patients with a post baseline maximum value for ALT during the course of the study that were OORR high and were an increase from the baseline value. There was a single patient with an elevation of ALT to 18.5 x ULN with an elevation of bilirubin to 2.4 x ULN. L1111: ## Glucose
L1112: Examination of the ADBL dataset reveals the OORR high value for glucose is set at 200mg/dL. No OORR low limit value is assigned in the STDM or ADAM dataset. The variable LBNRIND (reference range indicator) has only 3 "LOW" entries for post baseline glucose values. The values represented as low are 24mg/dL, 29mg/dL and 32mg/dL. Above 40 mg/dL the variable entries are "NORMAL". Normal entries begin at 40mg/dL and remain normal until above values of 200mg/dL where "HIGH" is entered. "Normal" includes 146 entries from 95 patients with value between 40mg/dL and 60mg/dL with the distribution and counts shown in Figure 60. Based on the examination in the ADBL dataset the reference range values for blood glucose appear to be 40mg/dL for the lower boundary and 200mg/dL as the upper boundary.
L1113: **Figure 60 C021, ADBL dataset for Glucose, Distribution of Entries for Glucose Values from 40mg/dL up to and including 60mg/dL with Mean and Median.**
L1114: The unusually broad reference range for glucose is cause for close scrutiny of this laboratory parameter. The glucose dataset is examined with reference range boundaries set by the reviewer as 65mg/dL for the lower boundary and 150mg/dL for the upper boundary. The upper boundary is above the range for fasting glucose and above typical 2 hour post prandial glucose because the prandial status of patients at each study clinical chemistry sample may vary.

L1148: ## **Reviewer Comment, hypophosphatemia:**
L1149: There was a notably higher frequency of minimum study value OORR low phosphate than OORR high phosphate with a proportion of 20.3% and 12.2% respectively. The frequency of extreme low outliers was also greater than extreme high outliers. The low values do not have a consistent temporal relationship to initiation of cenobamate treatment and do not manifest as a sequential declining trend. Examination of the adverse events from among the patients with any minimum value &lt; 2\text{meq/L} does not reveal preferred terms clearly associated with hypophosphatemia, however, CNS dysfunction is frequent but is not easily differentiated from the trend of CNS dysfunction seen across cenobamate treatment. There does not appear to be a detectable clinical impact of the trend toward low phosphate. There were no critical values &lt; 1\text{meq/L}.
L1150: ## *Chemistry Reviewer Summary*
L1151: Core issues of concern were present for the parameters ALT, Glucose, Potassium and hypophosphatemia. Detailed exploration of ALT revealed a notable excess of OORR high post baseline maximum values than OORR low post baseline minimum values. The frequency of maximum post baseline values greater than 3 x ULN was 1.7% (22 patients). There was a single notable case with elevation of ALT and bilirubin to Hy's law levels, however this patient had a biliary tract obstructive lesion with resolution of the laboratory abnormalities post-surgery, Figure 58. There were no Hy's law cases. Examination of post baseline glucose value revealed a higher frequency of OORR low glucose values compared to OORR high. The ADAE dataset did not contain typical reference range limits so the boundaries for exploration were set as 65mg/dL to 150mg/dL by the reviewer. Although there were excess OORR low values observed the magnitude and frequency of change from baseline were similar. The low values were generally irregular and sporadic. There are no clear clinical implications of the sporadic post baseline OORR low observations. Examination of potassium values reveals a consistent trend of post baseline increase of potassium values with several extreme outliers. There were no concurrent adverse effects identified that may be related to hyperkalemia. The findings are in alignment with those seen for post baseline potassium values in the controlled dataset. An excess of low phosphate was also seen during the chemistry parameter review. There were no identifiable associated adverse effects and the OORR low values were generally irregular and sporadic in occurrence. The clinical implications of the low phosphate values are unclear. A
L1152: trend of decreasing globulin was also present but without identifiable association with adverse effects.

L1167: ## Reviewer Comment, DB Vital Signs:
L1168: The frequency of post baseline standing pulse values in the range of bradycardia or tachycardia do not show substantiate change from placebo and there is not does relationship for the frequency in either category of &lt;60 bpm or &gt;100 bpm. Examination of supine pulse reveals an increased frequency in the &lt;60 bpm category compared to standing but only a small differential from placebo at any dose and no dose response. Supine pulse &gt;100 bpm has the lowest frequency, this is expected due to absence of maintaining vertical posture. In this range of pulse there is no difference between treatment and placebo, Table 73.
L1169: The sponsor has provided a dataset with three categories of standing systolic blood pressure, Table 74. The categories are &lt;90mmHg, &gt;140mmHg, and &gt;160mmHg. In the category of post baseline standing systolic blood pressure &lt; 90mmHg there are a low frequency of patients. there is a small differentiation from placebo and a dose response. This observation may be due to a small effect of orthostatic blood pressure challenge, but the clinical implications are not certain due to the low frequencies observed. There are a high frequency of patients with a standing systolic blood pressure value &gt;140mmHg with no separation from placebo and no dose response. In the standing systolic blood pressure range &gt; 160mmHg the frequencies are low in all arms with no separation from placebo and no dose response.
L1170: Standing diastolic pressure is also examined, Table 75. In the range of diastolic pressure &lt; 50mmHg the frequency was low with no dose response, the highest frequency (1.8%) was seen in the 400mg treatment arm at over 3 times the frequency in placebo. In the range of &gt;90mmHg the highest frequency was in the placebo arm with no dose response seen in the cenobamate treatment arms although all had a lower frequency than placebo. In the range &gt;100mmHg the frequency drops with the highest frequency in placebo and a dose response of declining frequency in the cenobamate treatment arms.
L1171: The trend seen in standing systolic and diastolic blood pressure is consistent with a trend of reduced value in the cenobamate treatment arms more prominent in the 400mg arm. Examination of the adverse event dataset reveals one episode of syncope in the 200mg treatment arm, there are no entries for blood pressure decreased and three entries for tachycardia with 0.4% in the 200mg treatment arm and 0.5% in the 400mg treatment arm. The small shift to higher frequencies in the lower blood pressure categories do not translate in to detection of a clinical signal in the adverse event profile of the DB adverse event dataset.
L1172: Body weight change is examined using the sponsors categorical vital signs dataset, frequencies by dose arm and weight change category are generated, Table 76. Change in body weight from baseline to treatment is examined for frequencies of patients with an increase or decrease of \ge 7\%. The frequency of those patients with a decline in weight from baseline there was no difference between placebo and the 100mg or 200mg treatment arms. There was an increase frequency in the 400mg treatment arm over placebo by 3.5%. In the category of gain of \ge 7\% there is an increase over placebo in all cenobamate treatment arms with the largest increase over placebo in the 400mg treatment arm. As a comparator, the weight trend in the placebo arm from baseline to end of treatment was examined. There was a mean gain of 3.7% across
L1173: 213 patients with available measurements. In addition, one hundred eleven (111, 52%) patients had a weight gain while 94 (44%) patients had a weight loss and 8 (3.8%) had no change. This analysis reveals a small positive weight shift in placebo. Overall the weight gain trend in the cenobamate treatment arms does not reach the threshold for an adverse effect in section 6 of labeling.

L1194: ## Systolic Blood Pressure
L1195: ## &lt;90mmHg
L1196: There were 42 (3.1%) patients identified with any post baseline systolic blood pressure &lt;90mmHg. The profile of values from visit 3 to 14 is examined to assess the frequency of study measurements with a value less than 90mmHg. There were a total 489 of post baseline measurements from visit 3 to 14 (post baseline). From among these visits there were 422 (86%) measurements with values ≥90mmHg and 68 (14%) with values &lt; 90mmHg. This indicates that a small proportion of values from these 42 patients were below 90mmHg during the course of the study. It is of interest to determine if there was a temporal relationship of the low value to
L1197: 18 Vital sign measurements in study C021 are obtained after 5 minutes in the supine position
L1198: the start of cenobamate treatment and also how the proportion of low BP measurements (&lt;90\text{mmHg}) was distributed across the study visits.
L1199: Examination of the distribution of the 42-patient subset, group measurements, by study visit is as granular as an individual patient by patient profile of each patient's blood pressure. Such an approach for a determination of whether a small number of patients had persistent sub 90\text{mmHg} value at each visit and reveals the magnitude of the individual values. However, the group distribution approach does provide an assurance that a majority of patients do not have a &lt;90\text{mmHg} value at a large proportion of visits. Figure 67 reveals that the proportion of patients with a blood pressure decline &lt;90\text{mmHg} increases at study visit 6 (mean study day 62) but does not exceed 23 percent of patients at any study visit. The lowest group mean blood pressure is seen at visit 13 (mean study day 272) when 37 of the initial 42 patients have measurements obtained. The profile of these systolic blood pressure outliers is consistent with the occurrence of sporadic elevations below 90\text{mmHg}. The group mean values of all measurements (*above and below the 90\text{mmHg} threshold*) are &gt;100\text{mmHg} systolic pressure at all visits except number 13 where the mean is 99\text{mmHg}.

L1206: ## **Reviewer Comment, Blood pressure:**
L1207: The profile of outlier patients with systolic blood pressure values &lt;90mmHg or &gt;160mmHg show these events are primarily due to sporadic elevations and they occur in small subsets, approximately 3% of patients in the high and low outlier groups. This analysis does not reveal a signal for widespread sustained depression of elevation of blood pressure.
L1208: ## **Weight**
L1209: An outlier (YKP3089C021-(b)(6)) is identified with an increase of weight from 60.8 kg, day 30, to 141.3kg, day 217, in 187 days. This would equal a daily weight gain in that interval of 0.43kg (.95lbs). The weight then drops to 66.4kg by day 372 (155 days). This equals a decline of 0.48kg /day. The plausibility of this weight gain and loss profile is uncertain. The patient has an adverse event of “depressed mood”. This patient is excluded from the remainder of the study group analysis.
L1210: The proportion of patients with a maximum post baseline weight increase of \ge 7\% or have a decline \le 7\% are captured. There were 118 (9.3%) patients that had an increase of \ge 7\% while there were 172 (13.6%) patients with a post baseline decline that was greater than or equal to 7%.
L1211: In the group with \ge 7\% weight loss there were 48 (3.8%, n=1262) patients with a loss of more than 10kg (22lbs). The group mean weight loss of the 172 patients was -14.7kg with a median of -12.7 kg and a maximum loss of -37kg. The cohort with a \ge 7\% weight gain there were 17 (1.3%, n=1262) patients with greater than 10kg weight gain and the subset of 118 patients with an increase of \ge 7\% had a mean and median weight gain of 7.5kg and 6.5kg respectively with a maximum of 26kg (with above outlier excluded).
L1212: ## Reviewer Comment, weight:
L1213: There is an excess of weight loss over weight gain in the open label safety study that is divergent from the small excess of weight gain over weight loss seen in the controlled study data. The frequency and magnitude of weight loss over weight gain in this one year open label study reveal a potential signal for weight loss.
L1214: Reviewer Summary, Vital Signs: examination of pulse reveals an excess of supine pulse &gt; 60 bpm over a pulse &gt; 100 bpm. This is not divergent from expectations for supine resting pulse in a population with a mean age of 40 years old. Systolic blood pressure does not reveal a clinically meaningful signal for depression or elevation of blood pressure. There is a potential signal for weight loss, but this does not reach a threshold of likelihood the observations diverge from the background variability.

L1254: ## 8.4.11. QT
L1255: A thorough QT study was performed by the sponsor and evaluated by the IRT for QT studies. The summary from the final consult document is provided below. This study reveals that cenobamate shortened the QTc interval in a dose dependent manner with a mean reduction of -9ms at the 200mg dose and -16ms for a 500mg dose (one in a day). The IRT has also recommended post marketing non-clinical study to determine the anti-arrhythmic class (\text{Na}^+ channel blockade) of cenobamate to better understand potential risk, as noted in the summary below.
L1256: ## “Summary
L1257: Cenobamate shortened the QTc interval in a dose-dependent manner — the largest mean reduction in QTc was -9 ms for 200 mg QD and -16 ms for 500 mg QD. A decrease from baseline of more than 20 ms was observed in 31% subjects taking 200 mg and 66% taking 500 mg compared to 7.17% for placebo. There were 4% subjects taking 200 mg QD and 8% subjects taking 500 mg QD with QTc &lt;350 ms. The magnitude of QTc shortening is similar to BANZEL (rufinamide), another anti-epileptic drug, which carries a warning for QT shortening and we therefore recommend inclusion of a similar warning in the label for cenobamate. The shortening of the QTc interval is likely due to inhibition of the cardiac sodium channel, which raises potential safety concerns with its use in patients with structural heart disease and its potential to unmask Brugada syndrome. Therefore, we are recommending additional nonclinical studies to understand the type of sodium channel blockade (see our recommendation in section 2.1).
L1258: The effect of cenobamate was evaluated in Study YKP3089C020. The highest dose that was evaluated was 500 mg QD, which covers the therapeutic exposures with the highest clinical dose (400 mg QD). The data from Study YKP3089C020 was analyzed using central tendency as the primary analysis (refer to section 4.3) – see Table 1 for overall results. The findings of this analysis are further supported by the available nonclinical data (Appendix 5), exposure-
L1259: response analysis (section 4.5), and categorical analysis (section 4.4).
L1260: **Table 81 The Point Estimates and the 90% CIs (FDA Analysis)**
L1261: ECG Parameter	Treatment	Time	\Delta\Delta\text{QTcF} (ms)	90% CI (ms)
QTcF	Cenobamate 200 mg QD (day 35)	0.5h	-8.6	(-12.3, -5)
QTcF	Cenobamate 500 mg QD (day 63)	0.5h	-16.0	(-19.8, -12.3)
L1262: The observed, geometric mean of steady state Cmax at 200 mg QD and 500 mg QD dose levels are 23.1 ug/mL and 63.8 ug/mL, respectively. This is generally in alignment with previous observations at the same dose levels. According to the sponsor's conclusion on dose proportional increase in Cmax,ss (summary of clinical pharmacology), the 500 mg QD dose (i.e., the maximum tolerated dose in healthy subjects) in this TQT study provided approximately 1.25-fold exposure margin for the maximum therapeutic dose (i.e., 400 mg QD). There are no known intrinsic or extrinsic factors (i.e., sex, age, race, food, mild to severe renal impairment, or mild to moderate hepatic impairment) that substantially increase cenobamate exposures. The effect of severe hepatic impairment on cenobamate exposure has not been evaluated and the proposed label does not recommend its use in these patients."

L1268: Entries from the Phase 1 ISS are examined. In the set with SAFFL 1 and SAFFL 2 affirmative there were examined. There were 2006 adverse event entries from 328 patients from 20 studies (Table 82), under 311 preferred terms.
L1269: ## *Death*
L1270: There was a death in study YKP3089C020 the TQT study. Patient YKP3089C020-(b) (6) developed an eosinophilic myocarditis due to DRESS and had a fatal outcome. This case is entered in Section 8.4.1 and Submission Specific Safety Issues, Section 8.6.1, DRESS Risk.
