L1276: ## Reviewer Comment, SAE:
L1277: Examination of the SAEs in the phase 1 safety data reveal serious hypersensitivity events that may reflect the rapid cenobamate dose escalation at the time in the product development. These events also demonstrate the powerful pro-hypersensitivity potential of cenobamate, underscored by case YKP3089C018- (b)(6) (discontinuation) where signs and symptoms of a hypersensitivity syndrome began 7.8 hours after the 1st dose of cenobamate and case YKP3089C016- (b)(6) (SAE) where the hypersensitivity reaction began less than 4 hours after the 1st dose of cenobamate. In both of these cases the initial dose of cenobamate was 200mg and both had a 7 to 10-day course from onset to recovery. Intense CNS adverse effect is seen in a patient with characteristics of intoxication of sufficient severity to cause a fall. The report of spontaneous abortion where the fetus was likely exposed in the 1st 2 to 4 weeks of development have an uncertain relationship to cenobamate.

None was an SAE and none resulted in cenobamate withdrawal.

L1290: ## Reviewer Comment:
L1291: Examination of the phase 1 study TEAEs, a cohort of subjects without prior exposure to and adaptation to antiepileptic drugs reveals that CNS adverse effects are the most prominent. Headache and gastrointestinal adverse effects are also frequent. There is a high frequency of “Orthostatic heart rate response increased” (2%). This is pooled with Postural orthostatic tachycardia syndrome”, and “dizziness postural” to yield a frequency of 4.8%. This points to a signal for change in peripheral vascular resistance, however, none of these events was an SAE or resulted in study drug withdrawal.
L1292: Adverse events associated with hypersensitivity with a frequency of 2% or greater are the preferred terms “dermatitis contact” and “feeling hot” where “feeling hot” was tested for concurrent association with events in the “Skin and subcutaneous tissue disorders”. These findings are in alignment with the pro-hypersensitivity characteristics of cenobamate.

L1297: ## Hematology
L1298: ## Hemoglobin
L1299: There were 173 instances from 48 subjects with normal baseline hemoglobin value that shifted to OORR low post baseline. The maximum negative shift was 2.1g/dl from a baseline of 13.4g/dl and the minimum overall value was 10.1 g/dl, a negative change from a baseline of 11.6 g/dl. The largest magnitude negative change from baseline was -15.7%. Mean and median percent change from baseline from among those with a post baseline OORR low value were -3.8% and -4.4% respectively. The distribution of change from baseline reveals that 9 of the 48 subjects had a positive percent change from baseline. There were 5 values from 3 subjects with a minimum value &lt; 11g/dl.
L1300: ## Reviewer Comment, Hemoglobin:
L1301: Overall there is a signal for shift to post baseline OORR low hemoglobin values. There were 3 subjects with values between 11 and 10g/dl. No critical values were reached. The basis for the shift is uncertain. This may be related to study phlebotomy but a relation to cenobamate cannot be excluded.
L1302: ## Leukocytes
L1303: There were 31 instances from 15 subjects with normal baseline leukocyte value that declines to OORR low. The minimum value from among these events is 2.8 \times 10^9 cells/L while the maximum percent change from baseline is -51%. The mean leukocyte count from this OORR low instances is 3.4 \times 10^9 cells/L and the mean percent change from baseline is -28%. The overall mean percent change of all instances of leukocyte post baseline, on treatment measurements is -8.1%. Three subjects, YKP3089C020-[REDACTED](b)(6), YKP3089C018-[REDACTED](b)(6), YKP3089C018-[REDACTED](b)(6) with lowest post baseline values of 2.8 \times 10^9 cells/L, 2.9 \times 10^9 cells/L and 3.0 \times 10^9 cells/L respectively had all leukocyte values obtained during study examined.
L1304: There were 10 instances from 6 subjects with normal baseline leukocyte value that had a post baseline increase to an OORR high value. The maximum percent change from baseline was 44% to a value of 13.8 \times 10^9 cells/L.
L1305: The subject from study C020 (TQT)- YKP3089C020-[REDACTED](b)(6) remained in reference range after 30 days of treatment, then the low value emerged at approximately 60 days of treatment. This value improved in dechallenge interval of 5 days to 3.2 \times 10^9 cells/L. The next subject, YKP3089C018-[REDACTED](b)(6) had a decline to 2.9 \times 10^9 cells/L at 1st post baseline measurement at day 31. This rebounded to normal range after a 6-day dechallenge. The third subject, YKP3089C018-[REDACTED](b)(6), also from study C018 had a similar pattern with post baseline decline on day 31 to 3.0 \times 10^9 cells/L then a rebound to 6.0 \times 10^9 cells/L after a 6-day dechallenge.
L1306: ## Reviewer Comment, leukocytes:
L1307: There is a small signal for a decline in leukocytes associated with cenobamate treatment. In the majority of cases the decline is not below a threshold of 3.0 \times 10^9 cells/L.
L1308: ## Neutrophils
L1309: There were 18 instances from subjects with normal baseline neutrophil count that declined to OORR low at any post baseline measurement while on cenobamate treatment. The minimum value recorded in 0.9 \times 10^9 cells/L, all other OORR low values ranged between 1.1 \times 10^9 cells/L and 1.8 \times 10^9 cells/L. The mean value of all 18 OORR low instances was 1.44 \times 10^9 cells/L. The maximum % change from baseline was -31% in the subject who had the nadir of 0.9 \times 10^9 cells/L. The mean percent change of all instances of decline to OORR low was -34.5%. The lowest neutrophil value occurred in subject YKP3089C018-(b) (6) in study C018 occurred on treatment day 31 (only treatment measurement). The subsequent value 6 days later rebounded to 1.5 \times 10^9 cells/L.
L1310: There were 6 instances from 6 subjects with a reference range neutrophil count that increased post baseline to an OORR high value. The maximum value was 11.6 \times 10^9 cells/L which represented a 123% increase over baseline.
L1311: The mean percent change of all 737 neutrophil post baseline, on treatment measurements was -11.5% with a maximum percent decline from baseline of 61%.
L1312: Two subjects with a negative mean change from baseline are selected to examine the course of neutrophil counts during the study timeline. These subjects are YKP3089C026-(b) (6) and YKP3089C020-(b) (6) with a mean change from baseline for all study measurements of -43% and -44.6% respectively. They also had 17 and 4 neutrophil measurements respectively. The results of the neutrophil value by study day and cenobamate dose for each of these two subjects is shown in Figure 71 and Figure 72.
L1313: Three subjects with the lowest neutrophil counts and change from baseline ranging from a minimum of 39% to a maximum of -61% are matched to the ADAE dataset to search for adverse events related to infection. One of the three, subject YKP3089C018-(b) (6) have 5 preferred term entries. None of these is from the "infections and infestations" SOC.

L1316: ## **Reviewer comment, Neutrophils:**
L1317: The post-baseline decline to OORR low values was in excess of the shift to OORR high values. The mean percent change of all 737 post baseline measurements from all subjects across the 5 studies was a decline of 11.5% from baseline value. Examination of neutrophil counts by patient across study measurements does not reveal a sustained decline in values. There is also no decline to critical neutropenia. There is a signal for decline in neutrophil values. the decline does not appear to be progressive, and it is rapidly reversible. A sample examination of the relationship between adverse events and the subjects with the lowest neutrophil counts does not reveal an occurrence of infections than would point to an effect on immunocompetence.
L1318: ## **Platelets**
L1319: Examination of the post baseline platelet counts reveals were 20 instances from 10 subjects. From among these there were 9 instances from three patients with baseline platelet values &lt; 150 \times 10^9 /L. In each of these cases there was a further decline from the OORR low baseline. The additional declines ranged from -13% to -18%. Among all OORR low values all but one were a decline from baseline. The mean change from baseline of all OORR low entries (post baseline) was -15%.
L1320: Subjects with OORR low post baseline values have all measurements examined. The number of samples is limited in most studies to draw conclusions on a sustained trend. Studies AA24143 and YKP3089C009 provide a baseline and single on treatment post baseline value (the OORR low post-baseline). Study YKP3089C020 provides a baseline, 2 on treatment and a day 1 post treatment value. This is useful for trend examination. In this study there are five subjects with an OORR low platelet count. Three of the five subjects have a trendline with a negative slope while two have a positive slope. In one subject all but one value including eh baseline value is below 162,000. All subjects oscillate between increasing and decreasing post baseline values while in no case is there a sequential decline in platelet count at each successive measurement.
L1321: Examination of subjects in study YKP3089C026 have 16 post baseline platelet measurements over a period of 92 days. Two patients (YKP3089C026-(b)(6) YKP3089C026-(b)(6)) in this study had OORR low post baseline values. all platelet values are examined over the study timeline of these patients. this analysis reveals a trend for decreasing platelet value that is aligned with duration of exposure and increasing dose over the 92-day exposure span. Decline is not consisted with some oscillation around the trendline but on overall pattern of decline is present. In neither case does the platelet nadir drop to a critical value.
L1322: The overall trend of platelet counts in study C026 is examined due to the timeline and number of platelet measurements. Cenobamate dosing spans 12.5mg to 200mg during the 92-day study timeline. This extended timeline, dosing and measurement frequency will inform the behavior of platelet values. The mean percent change from baseline for each of the 24 subjects is calculated. There is a higher frequency of positive means than negative means. The distribution
L1323: is shown in Figure 73. The mean of all subject mean percent change from baseline is +1.0%. The minimum post baseline platelet count is 121 \times 10^9 / \text{L} and 6 entries have values &lt;140 \times 10^9 / \text{L}. Those entries are from the two patients discussed in the above paragraph.
L1324: Figure 73 Phase 1, Platelets, Study C026, Mean % Change from Baseline by Subject
L1325: ## Reviewer Comment, platelets:
L1326: There are outlier subjects that have a trend of declining platelet count to OORR low values. Platelet values do not decline to a range of clinical concern or critical values. Overall, among the studies examined the frequency of decline in platelet value from baseline is greater than frequency of increase over baseline by a small margin. The analyses performed do not reveal a signal of concern for decline in platelet count.
L1327: ## Reviewer Comment, Hematology:
L1328: Hemoglobin, leukocytes, neutrophils and platelet values from 5 phase 1 studies in healthy subjects are examined. These studies were multiple dose studies with intervals not associated with concomitant medications. There was a trend toward a small decline with duration of treatment and increasing dose for all four parameters. The decline was not severe, critical thresholds were not reached, decline was inconsistent rather than sustained, and where dechallenge was observed at the end of study treatment values rapidly increased. The analyses do not reveal a safety signal for these hematology parameters.
L1329: ## Chemistry
L1330: ALT: there were 39 entries from 5 patients with ALT values &gt;100 \text{ IU/L}. Twenty (20) of these entries were from subject YKP3089C020-(b) (6) and 14 are from subject YKP3089C018-
L1331: (b) (6) Both of these subjects experienced DRESS syndrome and thus occur in the context of a severe hypersensitivity reaction. When subject (b) (6) and (b) (6) are excluded there are 5 remaining instances of ALT that are OORR high. Patient YKP3089C026- (b) (6) had a normal baseline with elevation to OORR high on study day 27. Values thereafter remained frequently OORR high over a period of 139 study days, with a peak on study day 68. L1332: Two events from study C016 are considered from subject YKP3089C016- (b) (6) who also experienced a hypersensitivity syndrome.
L1333: ## Reviewer Comment, ALT:
L1334: DRESS syndrome that occurred in two patients accounted for 87% of all instances of OORR high ALT that were observed in the 5 phase 1 studies that were reviewed. From among the remaining instances, one subject had recurrent, sporadic elevations to OORR high. All other occurrences to OORR high were sporadic. The review does not reveal a notable signal for elevation of ALT among the healthy phase 1 subjects.
L1335: Alkaline Phosphatase: there were 16 entries from 4 patients with ALP values &gt;132. A value of 132 IU/L is choosing because this is the highest upper boundary from among all entries for OORR high. From among these 4 patients the highest values (max. 218 IU/L) are seen in subject YKP3089C020- (b) (6) who had the severe DRESS reaction with a fatal outcome. From among the remaining 3 patients the maximum value is 186 IU/L, 1.4 x ULN. There is no signal for alkaline phosphatase elevation.
L1336: AST: there were 10 entries from 4 patients with an AST value. 2 x ULN. 7 of these 9 entries were from patient YKP3089C020- (b) (6) who had the severe DRESS reaction with a fatal outcome. From among the remaining three patients, one also had a diagnosis of DRESS on study day 14, with a maximum AST value of 173 (3.7 x ULN) on treatment day 24. This patient had cenobamate discontinued on treatment day 22. Subsequent AST values on treatment days 34 and 37 were within reference range. The next patient considered, YKP3089C020- (b) (6) had an elevation of AST to 3.2 x ULN on treatment day 78. This was 13 days after the last dose of cenobamate 500mg. An unscheduled measurement on study day 89 (post treatment day 23) revealed a value of 23 IU/L (.5 x ULN). The final patient, YKP3089C026- (b) (6) from among the four patients with AST elevations &gt; 2 \times ULN had excursions to 1.4 x ULN and 2.1 x ULN on cenobamate treatment days 20 and 23 respectively where dose was increased from 12.5mg to 25mg on treatment day 20.
L1337: ## Reviewer Comment, AST:
L1338: Examination of AST values reveals an increase associated with DRESS. The remaining OORR high events were sporadic, not sustained, and intermittent. A single delayed elevation at 23 days post treatment is sporadic. Overall there is no signal for elevation of AST values.
L1339: Bilirubin: There were 4 entries from 2 subjects with a normal baseline bilirubin value that shifted to OORR high at a post baseline measurement. None of these values exceeded 2 x ULN. One of these patients was YKP3089C020-(b) (6) who had DRESS syndrome with a fatal outcome. The maximum value in this case was 2.3 mg/dl (1.44 x ULN). In the remaining case, subject YKP3089C020-(b) (6) had a maximum total bilirubin value of 2.7 mg/dl (1.7 x ULN). This elevated value occurred on cenobamate treatment day 66, the day after last dose of 500mg cenobamate with no elevation of ALT, AST or Alkaline phosphatase. The subject also had elevation of direct bilirubin. The only adverse event entry in the ADAE dataset is constipation on study day 64. Two subsequent measurements were obtained on post treatment day 12 and 13 with bilirubin values of 1.8 (1.12 x ULN) and 1.7mg/dl (1.06 x ULN) respectively.
L1340: ## Reviewer Comment, Bilirubin:
L1341: Elevations to OORR high were associated with a severe hypersensitivity syndrome or were sporadic. Patient YKP3089C020-(b) (6) had elevation in both direct and total bilirubin with no concurrently associated laboratory abnormalities. The reason for this outlier is uncertain but does not appear to be a case of hepatocellular injury. Overall there is no signal for increase of bilirubin.
L1342: Bicarbonate: one non-baseline or screening bicarbonate value was OORR high. This was very close to reference range at 1.03 x ULN. There two patients with a normal baseline that had a decline to OORR low bicarbonate. One of these patients had a value that was 0.1mmol/L below RR at study day 17 and the second had a decline from baseline of 5.2mmol/L at study day 24 with a subsequent value within reference range at study day 37.
L1343: ## Reviewer Comment, bicarbonate:
L1344: There is no evidence of pathologic variation of bicarbonate.

L1348: Creatinine: In study AA24143 there are 6 (17%) subjects in the cenobamate treatment cohort with normal baseline creatinine that have an increase in creatinine value at study day 15, the day following final cenobamate dose. Creatinine values consistently decline toward baseline at post dose measurements. L1349: study YKP3089C009 with an elevation in creatinine at end of cenobamate (300mg) dosing and none in the placebo cohort. There were none with this pattern in the inactive treatment group. In study C020 (TQT study) there were 8 subjects with an increase from normal baseline to an OORR high creatinine value during cenobamate treatment. This elevation was not sustained and in 5 cases improved while on continued cenobamate treatment. In two cases the maximum value occurred after cessation of cenobamate, subjects YKP3089C020- (b) (6) (11% over baseline) and YKP3089C020- (b) (6) (13% over baseline). In study YUKP3089C026 there were 2 subjects with an elevation in creatinine over normal baseline. In both these cases the elevation normalized while cenobamate treatment was continued.
L1350: ## **Reviewer Comment, Renal:**
L1351: Examination of BUN revealed 7 cases with an elevation during cenobamate dosing. Evaluation of creatinine values in these 7 cases revealed the same trend. Subsequent examination of creatinine values revealed 20 subjects with a normal baseline creatinine and a post baseline excursion to OORR high. This trend was most consistent in study AA24143 while in study YKP3089C020 there were elevations of creatinine that improved as cenobamate treatment continued. In two cases a maximum value was recorded post treatment. The shift from the upper limit of normal generally did not exceed 15%. The implication of this observation is uncertain. The magnitude of increase in these phase 1 studies coupled with the observation of very uncommon sustained sequence of elevated values does not appear to be a signal in the pivotal studies for renal dysfunction associated with cenobamate treatment.
L1352: The sponsor also identifies this signal in the phase 1 population with the following entry in the ISS section 5.2.4 Clinical Chemistry, Phase 1 Pool:
L1353: The most frequent abnormal post-baseline clinical chemistry parameters observed in ≥50 subjects include the following:
L1354: - Glucose: 70/401 (17.5%) subjects
- Creatinine: 61/401 (15.2%) subjects
L1355: There were no clinically important signs and symptoms associated with these abnormal post-baseline clinical chemistry parameters.
L1356: **C-Reactive Protein:** C reactive protein is captured in study YKP3089C026. There were 4 post baseline OORR high values. Examination of all values in the study reveals these abnormal values are sporadic and not part of a sustained or escalating trend associated with cenobamate dosing.
L1357: **Calcium:** Calcium values are examined. there were 4 entries for 4 patients with a single elevation of calcium OORR high. These were sporadic values not part of a sustained or
L1358: escalating trend associated with cenobamate dosing. There were 7 entries from 4 patients. These were also sporadic values on the study timeline.
L1359: **Chloride:** There were 5 post baseline OORR low chloride values from 4 subjects. The trend for these subjects was examined and these values were found to be isolated or sporadic or both. There were no OORR High chloride values.
L1360: ## Creatinine Phosphokinase (CPK, CK)
L1361: There were 35 entries from 20 subjects in 7 phase 1 studies with on active cenobamate treatment with a post baseline CK value &gt; 400 IU/L. The maximum CK value was 4434 in study C026.
L1362: The adverse event dataset of these subjects is examined for related terms. There were 55 adverse event from 34 preferred terms. None was entered as an SAE, all were judged “mild” or “moderate” severity. There was one drug withdrawal due to and event of “Subcutaneous abscess”. There were 7 subjects with an entry for “Blood creatine phosphokinase increased”. There was one entry each for the muscle related terms “muscle spasms”, “musculoskeletal discomfort” and “muscle twitching”.
L1363: Examination of the complete profile of CK entries for the 20 subjects with identified CK &gt; 400 IU/L reveals that recordings of elevate CK are sporadic, occur on a background of high pre-treatment values, or occur at the lowest cenobamate dose then return to normal as dose increases. There were 3 patients with a post dose elevation followed by decline after cessation, see Table 88.
L1364: ## Reviewer Comment, Serum CPK:
L1365: Detailed examination of the CK elevations in the phase 1 studies reveals 3 instances that are related to cenobamate treatment, have a directional trend to related to treatment or dechallenge response to cessation of treatment. The majority are sporadic events or do not have clear coupling to cenobamate treatment. In one case there is an inverse relationship to dosing where the CK level declines as cenobamate dose increases. Although on initial exam the frequency of elevated serum CK values caused concern, close examination of the CK values of each measurement for all patients does not reveal a signal for cenobamate related CK elevation.

L1368: ## **Glucose:**
L1369: Examination of glucose values in the Phase 1 safety dataset (Table 86) are examined. In subjects with normal baseline glucose values there were 17 entries from 14 patients with a post baseline glucose value OORR high or low, based on a composite reference range of 70mg/dl to 110mg/dl for the select studies. Four (1.6%) patients had an OORR low value while 10 (4.0%) patients had an OORR high value. The maximum shift to high was a value of 122 from a baseline of 106 while the maximum low shift was to a value of 63 from a baseline of 97. A scatterplot with trendline of each of these 14 patients is examined. Eight patients have a positive slope where in 3 cases there is a clear systematic trend. Six patients have a negative slope across study glucose values. Both the high and low shift from baseline of greatest magnitude were 35%.
L1370: ## **Reviewer Comment, Glucose:**
L1371: There is a small excess of elevation of glucose over shift to low glucose. There is a small signal for glucose elevation that does not reach a level of clinical concern.
L1372: **Potassium:** Examination of potassium values reveals an excess of shift from normal baseline to OORR low. There were 27 instances from 14 (5.7%) subjects with an OORR low post baseline potassium value and 1 (0.4%) subject with a normal baseline and subsequent post baseline OORR high value. There was 1 patient with a shift to 3.1meq/L from 3.9 meq/L. The remaining 13 subjects had shifts to 3.4 meq/L or higher. Three subjects with potassium of &lt; 3.5 meq/L are examined for the potassium value trend across the study timeline. In two subjects the low potassium value was sporadic while in the remaining subject (YKP3089C018-[REDACTED](b)(6)) there was a consistent trend across measurements. This latter subject was withdrawn from on study day 1 due to a hypersensitivity reaction.
L1373: ## **Reviewer Comment: potassium:**
L1374: Overall, there was an excess of low post-baseline outliers. There is a signal for a cenobamate influence to reduce potassium values, but the effect is not manifest as a strong consistent trend and does not lead to critically low values.
L1375: **Sodium:** Examination of post-baseline, on treatment sodium values from the selected phase 1 studies reveals no OORR high values. There were 6 OORR low values from 5 subjects. 4 of these subjects had a value of 134 meq/L while one subject (YKP3089C018-[REDACTED](b)(6)) had a value of 122meq/L. This latter subject also had an SAE due to DRESS and was discontinued from the study. The shift to low of 134 from the low boundary of reference range 135meq/L is small.
L1376: ## **Reviewer Comment, sodium:**
L1377: Overall there is no signal for hyponatremia due to cenobamate treatment in healthy subjects.
L1378: ## **Reviewer Comment, chemistry:**
L1379: Areas of concern that were identified include elevation of ALT but only in the context of and independently driven hypersensitivity reaction rather than a primary DILI. Assessment of AST
L1380: did not identify a signal that reached a level of clinical concern. Sustained or sequentially escalating bilirubin levels were associated with a severe hypersensitive reaction. There was a shift to increase of creatinine in approximately 3% of patient. These measurements were generally sporadic and in one case after an elevation continued to decline as cenobamate dose increased. This observation does not reach the level of a signal of clinical concern. Frequent elevations of serum CPK were identified. Among these elevations the highest were sporadic without sequential elevation. No trend of sustained sequential CPK elevation was identified. Examination of glucose levels reveals a small trend to elevation with an excess of post baseline shift to OORR high values over shift to OORR low. The maximum values were not highly elevated OORR. There was no sustained sequential trend among subjects. Assessment of potassium revealed an excess of post baseline shift to OORR high over OORR low values. The effect was not manifest as a strong consistent trend and does not lead to critical low values.

L1408: ## ***Adverse Events Associated with Cessation of Cenobamate Treatment***
L1409: Cenobamate has mean elimination half-life ranged from 50 to 60 hours. This property may ameliorate withdraw signs and symptoms because the slow decay of cenobamate concentration allows time for adaptation to the loss of pharmacodynamic effects of the drug. However, withdrawal effects may occur. The phase 1 studies offer the opportunity to identify effects in a population of subjects that have not adapted to the chronic background central nervous system effects of Antiepilepsy drugs. Therefore, selected phase 1 studies were examined for features of drug withdrawal.
L1410: **Table 92 Phase 1 Studies Chosen to Examine for Withdrawal Adverse Events, Study ID, Dosing, Design, and Purpose**
L1411: STUDYID	dose	Design	purpose	peak dose	Study Duration	PBO n	YKP n	YKP Treatment Duration	post YKP Study day

YKP3089C009	MAD	PK 250-300mg	300	17	6	14	17	18

YKP3089C026	multiple	1 sequence	Interaction with P450 probe3	200	109	0	24	98	110
L1412: An analysis of adverse event potentially associated with cenobamate withdrawal was performed. Multiple dose phase 1 studies were selected that were did not have intervals of concomitant treatment with additional AEDs. Six studies were selected, these included AA24143, YKP3089C006, YKP3089C009, YKP3089C018, YKP3089C020, and YKP3089C026. There were 274 healthy subjects in these studies where the end day ranged from 14 days to 110 days, see Table 92. The phase 1 ISS, ADAE dataset was the source of adverse event preferred terms that were searched for potential withdrawal events. All adverse event entries that occurred on or after the treatment end date were captured from the six studies. This method captured 192 entries containing 105 preferred terms from 62 subjects that had an adverse event start date on or after the treatment end date where the treatment included cenobamate. The complete preferred term list is shown in Table 93.
L1413: From among the adverse events that were captured by this method a set of preferred terms were selected that may be associated with central nervous system stimulation or mood disorder. This category of adverse event yielded a group of 25 preferred terms from among the 92 instances of post treatment adverse effects. Adverse events that occurred later than 20 days from end of treatment are excluded. This exclusion yielded a final count of 45 adverse event entries from 25 subjects. These preferred terms with the number of subjects that experienced each of the adverse events and the time of adverse event from end of treatment is shown in Table 94.
L1414: The causality of adverse events that occurred on the last day of study drug treatment is less likely. The relationship of the time of last dose to time of event is important since the longer interval from last dose the greater likelihood the experience may be due to absence of the drug treatment. This fine structure of temporal relationship was not captured by the reviewer but day 0 events are captured.
L1415: The analysis revealed the most frequent events, with greater than 1 occurrence were the preferred terms “insomnia”, “headache”, “decreased appetite”, “depressed mood”, “discomfort”, “fatigue”, “feeling hot”, “head discomfort” and “tremor”. The term headache is very common in all safety datasets in the development program, therefore is very non-specific. The causal relationship of fatigue to drug withdrawal is weak but the non-specific nature of fatigue also allows for overlap with mood disorders and may be a surrogate for a depressed state. Single occurrence preferred terms that have a high likelihood of causal relationship to drug withdrawal are “body temperature increased”, “chills”, “heart rate increased”, “nervousness”, “nightmare”, and “tachycardia” because these events are seen with rebound of sympathetic nervous system, see Table 94.
L1416: An examination of time to onset of a given event may add to the causal strength of association. Events that occur in the more rapid phase of decline of cenobamate concentration are more suspect as withdrawal phenomena. All but two events occur within 8 days of cenobamate discontinuation. This represents approximately 3.2 half-lives of the drug, so these events are plausibly related to the decay of cenobamate concentration. The final two events that are listed in Table 94 occur 20 days after discontinuation and are less likely related to withdrawal.

L1420: ## Reviewer Comment, Withdrawal
L1421: The temporal relationship and the implied medial physiology of several preferred terms identified during the end of treatment interval are consistent with a cenobamate withdrawal effect. The terms most likely to have a causal relationship are “insomnia”, “anxiety”, “depressed mood”, “nightmare”, “decreased appetite”, “tremor”, “body temperature increased”, “chills”, “heart rate increased”, “nervousness”, “nightmare”, and “tachycardia”.
L1422: ## 8.5.1. Phase 1 Safety Summary
L1423: The most prominent SAEs related to cenobamate treatment were DRESS events. This identifies a pro-hypersensitivity property of cenobamate and emerged in the phase 1 studies were dose escalation was rapid. The remaining SAEs were unrelated to cenobamate. Examination of the phase 1 study TEAEs, a cohort of subjects without prior exposure to and adaptation to antiepileptic drugs reveals that CNS adverse effects are the most prominent. Headache and gastrointestinal adverse effects are also frequent. There is a high frequency of “Orthostatic heart rate response increased” (2%). This is pooled with Postural orthostatic tachycardia syndrome”, and “dizziness postural” to yield a frequency of 4.8%. This points to a signal for change in peripheral vascular resistance, however, none of these events was an SAE or resulted in study drug withdrawal. These events may be mechanistically related to the trend in vital signs where an increase in post dose heart rate and a small mean negative change in systolic blood pressure was observed in examination of study AA22780.
L1424: Adverse events associated with hypersensitivity with a frequency of 2% or greater are the preferred terms “dermatitis contact” and “feeling hot” where “feeling hot” was tested for concurrent association with events in the “Skin and subcutaneous tissue disorders”. These findings are in alignment with the pro-hypersensitivity characteristics of cenobamate. mate or uncommon outlier events of uncertain relationship.
L1425: Hemoglobin, leukocytes, neutrophils and platelet values from 5 phase 1 studies in healthy subjects are examined. These studies were multiple dose studies with intervals not associated with concomitant medications. There was a trend toward a small decline with duration of treatment and increasing dose for all four parameters. The decline was not severe, critical thresholds were not reached, decline was inconsistent rather than sustained, and where dechallenge was observed at the end of study treatment values rapidly increased. The analyses do not reveal a safety signal for these hematology parameters.
L1426: Areas of concern that were identified include elevation of ALT but only in the context of and independently driven hypersensitivity reaction rather than a primary DILI. Assessment of AST did not identify a signal that reached a level of clinical concern. Sustained or sequentially
L1427: escalating bilirubin levels were associated with a severe hypersensitive reaction. There was a shift to increase of creatinine in approximately 3% of patient. These measurements were generally sporadic and in one case after an elevation continued to decline as cenobamate dose increased. This observation does not reach the level of a signal of clinical concern. Frequent elevations of serum CPK were identified. Among these elevations the highest were sporadic without sequential elevation. No trend of sustained sequential CPK elevation was identified. Examination of glucose levels reveals a small trend to elevation with an excess of post baseline shift to OORR high values over shift to OORR low. The maximum values were not highly elevated OORR. There was no sustained sequential trend among subjects. Assessment of potassium revealed an excess of post baseline shift to OORR high over OORR low values. The effect was not manifest as a strong consistent trend and does not lead to critical low values. There was no shift trend or outlier effect seen on serum sodium.
L1428: Analysis of the phase 1 data for potential withdrawal adverse effects does reveal a positive signal. The temporal relationship and the implied medial physiology of several preferred terms identified during the end of treatment interval are consistent with a cenobamate withdrawal effect. The terms most likely to have a causal relationship are “insomnia”, “anxiety”, “depressed mood”, “nightmare”, “decreased appetite”, “tremor”, “body temperature increased”, “chills”, “heart rate increased”, “nervousness”, “nightmare”, and “tachycardia”.

L1432: C020 (TQT study) Subject YKP3089C020-[REDACTED] (b) (6)
L1433: Study C020 was the TQT study in healthy volunteers. Subject [REDACTED] (b) (6) was a 38 yo female who developed a rash on her face and neck with pruritis on study day 32 while taking 200mg of cenobamate. Study drug was discontinued on day 33. The subject was initially treated with diphenhydramine 25mg to 50mg up to three times a day. The subject reported several adverse events in the days and weeks after discontinuation from the study including, including depressed mood, face swelling, anxiety, back pain, alopecia, ecchymosis, blurred vision, chest pain, dyspnea, palpitations, headache, cough, fatigue, and asthenia. The depressed mood AE began 7 days after the last dose of study drug and the PI considered it possibly related to study drug. A text review of the course of events is presented in the following paragraphs. See Figure 77 for cenobamate exposure by study day. See Figure 78, Figure 79 and Figure 80 for a graphic overview of laboratory events by study day as well as the start of prednisone tape and day of death.

Vital signs and ECG parameters were within normal limits at the early termination event.
L1436: On study day 39 the subject returned to clinic and the rash was noted to be worse; a generalized maculopapular rash was now covering her face, neck, and trunk, anterior and posterior down to buttocks. The subject complained of pruritus and reported the diphenhydramine was not helping. In addition, she reported difficulty berating and felt the need to cough to breath better. L1438: She then returned to the clinic on study day 40 and reported no improvement in the rash or pruritus and had continued nausea. The ALT and AST continued to increase along with ALP, bilirubin, GGT and LDH.
L1439: On Study day 42 the subject had continued macular rash on chest, abdomen, back and legs with continued nausea. The subject had a dermatology assessment and the rash was noted to be diffuse, was itchy and red. She felt better after prednisone. A punch biopsy was performed, and the results were consistent with DRESS syndrome. The subject was continued on prednisone 20mg daily. On study day 44 the subject returned to clinic and the generalized erythropapular rash was worse and pronounced diffuse adenopathy was noted. The subject felt generally ill. Hepatic enzyme studies continued to worsen. Her ALT (937 I/U) and AST (610 I/U) were continuing to increase. Coagulation parameters were obtained and showed an elevated prothrombin time (PT, 13.5 seconds; reference range: 9.6 - 11.6 seconds) and international normalized ratio (INR, 1.3; reference range: 0.9 - 1.1). The subject then returned to the emergency room. The subject was followed by an internal medicine doctor and a hepatologist. While hospitalized, the subject continued receiving prednisone 60 mg and triamcinolone cream, hydroxyzine 25 mg, hydrocortisone cream, and diphenhydramine cream. She had a liver duplex ultrasound that was normal. Vital signs included blood pressures of 85 - 105/58 - 75 mmHg, pulse rates of 70s to 90s bpm, respiratory rate of 16 rpm, temperatures ranging from 36.7 to 38.1°C, and oxygen saturation of 94% on room air.
L1440: The subject's ALT and AST increased further while hospitalized, with maximum ALT of 1327 U/L and AST of 865 U/L on [REDACTED](b) (6) (Day 46). In addition to the elevated transaminases and out-of-range blood count differential parameters during the hospitalization, the subject's hemoglobin and hematocrit values were low, with hemoglobin values ranging from 10.9 to 11.8 g/dL (hospital reference range: 12.0 - 15.5 g/dL) and hematocrit values ranging from 32.7% to 34.7% (hospital reference range: 34.9% - 44.5%).
L1441: She was discharged to home on study day 46, with prescriptions for prednisone 60 mg with taper over the next 2 weeks, hydroxyzine 25 mg, triamcinolone cream, and diphenhydramine cream. On study day 49 the subject returned to clinic and hepatic enzymes remained elevated but were trending downward. The subject reported that the pruritus and rash were still present and follow up coagulation studies were within normal limits.
L1442: On study day 50 at a hepatology follow up visit the rash was dramatically improved. Hepatic enzymes were improving. Treatment at the time included prednisone 20mg daily but there were instructions to taper over 2 weeks. On study day 52 the subject returned to clinic. Lymphadenopathy was still present notably along the right posterior cervical area. She reported the swelling was improving. Hepatic enzyme elevation continued with ALT (1000 I/U) and AST (575 I/U).
L1443: On study Day 64, the subject returned to the clinic and reported the rash was improved but noted that sometimes it returned then went away. She also reported that since study day 57, she noticed facial swelling in the middle of her face and lips, which was worse upon waking in the morning. It had been progressively getting more noticeable and she reported that now the inside of her mouth was swollen. She denied shortness of breath or difficulty breathing.
L1444: Upon examination, her face and oral mucosa were noticeably swollen, and her swollen posterior cervical lymph node remained enlarged. On study day 68 the subject returned to clinic and was noted to have continued facial swelling, and a generalized maculopapular rash involving the trunk, neck, bilateral arms, and upper legs. The subject was started on a new course of prednisone. The subject reported feeling sad.
L1445: On study day 72 the subject was contacted by phone and reported that the rash, facial swelling, pruritus, and feeling sad continued with no improvement from yesterday. On study day 76, the clinic staff spoke to the subject on the phone. The subject stated that the facial edema and rash were now minimal, and that she was feeling less sad. On study day 80, the subject was contacted by phone and stated that she still had facial edema and was having feelings of sadness. She was concerned about losing so much hair. She stated she took her last dose of prednisone.
L1446: On study day 81, the subject was initially contacted by phone and stated she was having chest pain since the day before. The subject returned to the clinic, and reported chest pain with radiation to her back, that was accompanied by shortness of breath, palpitations, and blurred vision. The chest pain was still present but improved at the visit, and she was still having palpitations and mild shortness of breath. The subject also stated she was going to an emergency room after the clinic visit. Later that day, the clinic contacted the subject by phone and the subject stated her chest pain improved so she did not go to the emergency room. The clinic staff advised the subject to go to the emergency room, but she stated she would go if the chest pain worsened but she did not want to go at that time.
L1447: On study day 83, the subject returned to the clinic. Overall, her physical examination was much improved. She was noted to have mild edema to her cheeks and periorbital areas, and minimal rash to her bilateral forearms. The subject stated that she had increased stressors in her home life and finances over the past few days. The chest pain was better at the time of the visit, but she still noted some intermittent chest pain with exertion. She also reported a recent onset of fatigue. Vital signs included a blood pressure of 94/65 mmHg and pulse rate of 83 bpm. An ECG was within normal range, including a QTcF interval of 397 msec. Her liver enzymes were within normal range (ALT 43 I/U and AST 43 I/U), however, several WBC differential parameters were still elevated, including a WBC count of 11.3 thou/\muL and eosinophil percentage of 13.7%.
L1448: On study day 86, Subject (b) called into the clinic and reported ongoing chest pain. She stated that the chest pain was worse with movement. She denied shortness of breath but did state her chest hurt when she took a deep breath. She also reported ongoing stress with regard to family finances and the need to find childcare for her return visits. The subject was reminded again to go the emergency room for chest pain. The clinic staff suggested the subject try some relaxation techniques, such as taking a warm bath or listening to music. Later that evening she complained of chest pain to her husband, but it subsided.
L1449: On study day 87, the subject was found unresponsive by her husband and emergency medical services (EMS) were notified. She was transported to a local emergency room where she was pronounced dead. On [REDACTED](b)(6) a post-mortem examination was performed. The autopsy findings concluded that Subject [REDACTED](b)(6) died from a cardiac dysrhythmia due to eosinophilic myocarditis due to DRESS, with findings of pleural effusions, pericardial effusion, pulmonary edema, cardiomyopathy, and necrotizing myocarditis.

L1470: ## Reviewer Summary, DRESS Cases:
L1471: Subject YKP3089C020-(b) (6) developed the first hypersensitivity features on day 10 of 200mg/day dosing in a program with weekly escalation in 50mg increments. The second subject that develop DRESS, (YKP3089C018-(b) (6)) developed first DRESS features on day 4 of 400mg/day dosing after a total of 14 days of exposure to cenobamate. The precise dose and
L1472: exposure in the third case (YKP3089C017-(b) (6)) is not as clear. The patient was scheduled to by on 200mg for 15 days at time of first hypersensitivity features after treatment for a total of 23 days.
L1473: In cases YKP3089C020-(b) (6) and YKP3089C017-(b) (6) the titration schedules were similar, starting at 50mg and escalating by 50mg weekly. Case YKP3089C018-(b) (6) had the most rapid dose escalation starting at 100mg and advancing by 100mg weekly. All are more rapid than proposed dosing that begins at 12.5mg for two weeks before advancing to 25mg for two weeks.
L1474: In cases YKP3089C017-(b) (6) and YKP3089C018-(b) (6) the emergence of DRESS features was more immediate while in case YKP3089C020-(b) (6) there was a one-week interval of mild expression of DRESS. All three patients received corticosteroid therapy with clinical benefit noted. Subject YKP3089C020-(b) (6) appears to have a degree of relapse as prednisone was being tapered. A potential difference between the fatal case of DRESS (YKP3089C020-(b) (6)) and the other cases was the duration of cenobamate treatment. Case (b) (6) was treated for 32 days compared to 14 days and 23 days in the cases that resolved.
L1475: ## DNP Safety Review, Dress
L1476: A dedicated safety review of the DRESS risk and Study C021 was performed by the internal DNP safety reviewer.19 This review includes an assessment of the DRESS cases presented in the prior section. The reviewer concludes that though no additional DRESS cases were identified in study C021 but the data do not allow a definitive conclusion that the reduced cenobamate titration rate is solely responsible for the observed reduction in DRESS risk. This is because there were demographic differences between the Phase 1 trial that were the source of 2 of 3 DRESS cases as well as differences in exclusion criteria and pharmacovigilance measures employed across C021 and the earlier studies.
L1477: The safety reviewer concluded that a Medication Guide should be included with labeling that includes information like that provided to patients in study 021 about signs and symptoms of DRESS and instructions to contact health care providers immediately if these occur. The sponsor should closely monitor for and include as 15-day reports to the Agency any spontaneous reports suggestive of DRESS with the intention of assessing real world use risk and identification of predictive factors. In addition, the sponsor should perform genomic studies to look for predictive factors for DRESS. Also, the warning for DRESS in Section 5 of labeling should not imply that DRESS risk has been eliminated with the use of a lower starting dose and slower titration schedule.
L1478: 19 Gerard Boehm, ODE 1, DNP, 9/12/19:
https://darrts.fda.gov/darrts/faces/ViewDocument?documentId=090140af80515183&amp; afrmRedirect=395963667581783
L1479: ## *C021 Hypersensitivity Reports*
L1480: The first patient was screened for study C021 on [REDACTED](b) (6) but IND 76809 (YKP) was placed on partial clinical hold on 11/25/15 due to DRESS risk. Conditions of the partial hold did not allow new patient recruitment. Thereafter study C021 was modified to assess safety and DRESS risk using a titration schedule with reduced dose escalation rate, Table 96. The sample size was also expanded to cap the DRESS risk with the upper limit of the 95% confidence interval of 0.3% (given 0 as the point estimate of new DRESS cases). A continue partial hold letter was sent on 2/26/16 allowing conditional new patient recruitment. The conditions limited cenobamate dose to 200mg, an expansion of the already planned safety study C021 was required to “provide an adequate number of patients exposed for a minimum of six months to cap the risk of DRESS to a level acceptable for the proposed indication”. In addition, a monthly hypersensitivity report to include any hypersensitivity reactions reported by investigators, whether serious or not, was required to be submitted.

Day 1, 200mg
L1500: A 34 yo male subject was discontinued from the study because of a drug hypersensitivity reaction that began on Day 1, 7.8 hours after dosing with 200 mg YKP3089. The subject received 1 dose of 200 mg YKP3089 before dosing was discontinued.
L1501: On Day 1 (b) (6) the subject reported laying down to sleep after lunch and upon awakening noted erythema to his trunk, bilateral swelling of the eyelids, and a tight feeling in the face. He denied any welts or pruritus. Physical examination revealed no swelling of the uvula or tongue, and lungs were clear to auscultation. Vital signs remained stable. Medical assessment by the PI revealed a "diffuse confluent macular rash without papules, pustules or bullae involving predominantly the trunk and proximal extremities sparing mucosal sites, palms and soles." The rash had been noted by the subject to have progressed over the previous several hours. The PI decided not to administer any more doses of study drug. He denied pruritus and dyspnea and remained afebrile. The PI ordered oral diphenhydramine every 6 hours as well as a methylprednisolone tapering dose. Safety vital signs were also ordered to be done twice daily. These remained within normal limits. The subject remained confined at the clinic for continued assessment. Blood and urine tests done at this time to check for eosinophilia and kidney or liver involvement were all negative.
L1503: On Day 3 (b) (6) the facial swelling was resolved, and edema of the eye lids was significantly decreased. The diphenhydramine was discontinued as were the safety vital signs. Erythema of the bilateral legs was resolved. On Day 4 (b) (6) the subject was discharged from the clinic with orders to continue the methylprednisolone tapering dose and to call the clinic with any change in condition. At the early termination physical, the subject was noted to have slight erythema on legs as well as residual erythema to the bilateral eyelids.
L1504: Follow up phone calls were made with the subject to track the resolution of the AE. The erythema of the eyelids, trunk and legs as well as the edema of the eyelids continued to improve. On Day 8 (b) (6) the subject reported that the erythema of the legs had resolved. On Day 10 (b) (6) the subject reported that the erythema and edema of
L1505: the eye lids had resolved. The AE was considered resolved on Day 10. The PI considered this AE to be probably related to the study drug.
L1506: ## Measures to Reduce DRESS Frequency
L1507: Reduction of the rate of dose escalation and vigorous pharmacovigilance have been the approach to mitigate risk of DRESS. There is evidence in nonclinical model and also evidence from this approach in the clinical use of lamotrigine for support of slow titration in the reduction of hypersensitivity reactions.
L1508: In a nonclinical model of penicillamine induced autoimmunity a dose of 10mg/day for 2 weeks was shown to induce immune tolerance. In addition, the tolerance could be transferred to a naïve animal with spleen cell transfer.20 Clinical support for a strategy of dose reduction is found in experience with lamotrigine. In January 1994, the recommended starting dose of lamotrigine was lowered in an attempt to reduce the incidence of rash. After the starting dose was reduced. The incidence of serious rash was significantly reduced; however, no corresponding reduction in the overall incidence of lamotrigine-related skin rash was observed.21
L1509: ## DRESS Risk in Prospective
L1510: DRESS has been observed across several categories of therapeutic intervention in addition to Antiepilepsy drugs. Medications most commonly associated with DRESS syndrome in addition to AEDs are antibiotics (particularly beta-lactams), and allopurinol. Other medications that are known to be associated with DRESS include non-steroidal anti-inflammatory drugs, captopril, mood stabilizers, and antiretroviral drugs.
L1511: A search using the “FDALabel” tool for the occurrence of DRESS in Section 5 identifies 9 approved AEDs. In eight of the nine products there is a standard language heading within section five for the DRESS warning entry, shown in Table 97.

L1524: ## **Reviewer Comment, DRESS Risk**
L1525: The three cases of DRESS identify threat of a serious adverse event. In addition, the reports of early and intense hypersensitivity reactions reveal a propensity to dysfunctional immune system activation. However, it is not certain if the events identified in the section "Rapid Onset
L1526: Hypersensitivity Events" have the same mechanistic drivers as those underlying progression to DRESS syndrome or whether those hypersensitivity events forecast DRESS.
L1527: No additional DRESS cases occurred with the reduced titration rate in study C021. The study does not prove the strategy of slow escalation alone eliminates DRESS risk due to a specific mechanism, such as development of immune tolerance. However, the methodology of reduced titration and early identification of hypersensitivity signs and symptoms were successful in the study population and the outcome supports a risk level that is in alignment with a similar threat from SJS/TENS in lamotrigine. In addition, as shown in Table 98 and Table 99, this serious adverse event is present and managed in current mainstream AEDs and drug treatments in other therapeutic areas. As expressed in the safety review, labeling should include a medication Guide to provide patients with the same warning information on DRESS presentation.
L1528: ## 8.7. Safety Analyses by Demographic Subgroups

L1530: Examination of the distribution of the frequency of adverse events by SOC in males and females reveals the most notable difference occurs in the SOC “Gastrointestinal Disorders” where there is an excess of 10.1% in the proportion of females over males. In the remaining SOCs the differences are small and unlikely to represent a substantive clinical difference.

L1543: ## 8.8. Specific Safety Studies/Clinical Trials
L1544: ## C021
L1545: Study C021 was undertaken to evaluate the safety and pharmacokinetics (PK) of YKP3089 and concomitant antiepileptic drugs (AEDs) when administered as adjunctive therapy for the treatment of partial seizures. An additional purpose was to assess the risk of DRESS syndrome when cenobamate treatment is initiated at a reduced dose escalation relative to the phase 1 and pivotal studies. As noted in the discussion of DRESS risk in Section 2868.6.1 DRESS Risk there was a positive relationship seen in the development program between titration rate and hypersensitivity reactions.
L1546: The IND was placed on partial clinical hold due the occurrence of a two probable cases of DRESS and a case with a fatal outcome in the TQT study C020 on November 25, 2015. The partial clinical hold allowed continued treatment of patients on cenobamate treatment for ≥ 4 months, required that healthy volunteer subjects be tapered and discontinued with no new patients allow to enter into cenobamate treatment. In addition, there was requirement to explore the DRESS safety signal and identify measures to ensure patient safety.
L1547: In response to the partial clinical hold the sponsor proposed measures to prevent new cases of DRESS, to achieve early detection and evaluation of potential DRESS cases and ensure there is rapid reaction and treatment for any patient identified with DRESS.
L1548: A planned safety study C021 was underway at the time of the imposed partial clinical hold. In the “Partial Clinical Hold Complete Response” of 1/25/16 the sponsor proposed measures to satisfy the deficiencies identified in the “Partial Clinical Hold” of 11/25/15. These measures included the following items:
L1549: - Exclusion of patients with a history of any drug induced rash or hypersensitivity reaction
- Exclusion of patients with a history of a first degree relative that has had a serious cutaneous adverse reaction
- Reduce the rate of cenobamate dose escalation during titration
- Education of investigators to achieve a high level of awareness and capability to recognize DRESS
- Dispense a laminated card for study patients to carry that contains DRESS warning
L1550: information and instructions on obtaining appropriate treatment in the event a symptom is identified.
L1551: - A skin assessment diary
- Identification of local specialists that have expertise in DRESS proximate to each study site
- Measures to evaluate suspected DRESS cases to facilitate diagnosis in a timely manner. These measures included:Skin/Hypersensitivity assessment
- Physical examination/rash assessment
- Referral to local expert
- Notification of the safety advisory board
- Measures to institute care of patients who develop DRESSDrug withdrawal
- Medical specialist care
- Administration of steroids
L1552: The above measures were integrated into study C021 when the “Continue Partial Clinical Hold” letter allowing new patients or subjects to enter study with cenobamate treatment up to a dose of 200mg/day. This response was issued on 2/26/16 in response to the sponsor’s submission of 1/25/19.
L1553: During early 2017 discussion with our statistical team concluded that a study with a prespecified endpoint of 0 new cases of DRESS at the new modified reduced cenobamate dose escalation with a sample size of 1000 would be acceptable to limit the upper 95% CI to 0.3% DRESS frequency. The Division did have a concern that if any DRESS cases emerged in the study that it would fail to inform the question of upper boundary of DRESS frequency. Subsequently, as of April 23, 2018, a total of 1339 subjects received YKP3089 and were analyzed. The total includes 1110 subjects exposed for at least 6 months.
L1554: ## Study C021 Core Outline
L1555: **Objective:** The objective is to evaluate the safety and pharmacokinetics of YKP3089 and concomitant AEDs when administered as adjunctive therapy for the treatment of partial seizures. The evaluations will include:
L1556: - Phenytoin-YKP3089 interaction
- Phenobarbital-YKP3089 interaction
- Long term safety of YKP3089 as adjunctive therapy in subjects with partial onset seizures
- Population pharmacokinetics of YKP3089 and concomitant AEDs
L1557: **Methodology:**
L1558: This was a multicenter, open-label study in subjects with poorly controlled partial seizures consisted of a screening period, an open-label titration phase, an open-label maintenance phase, and for subjects discontinuing, a taper period and a follow-up visit.
L1559: The titration phase included 7 study visits (Visit 2 to Visit 8) and the maintenance phase included up to 6 visits in the first year (Visit 9 to Visit 14). During the first 16 weeks of treatment with YKP3089, visits occurred every 2 weeks. The follow-up visit (Visit 15) was to occur 14 days after last dose. Subjects who benefited from treatment may have continued beyond Year 1 with visits occurring every 3 months.
L1560: ## Design
L1561: This was a multicenter, open label study in subjects with poorly controlled partial seizures. Study structure included a screening period, OL irritation phase and OL maintenance phase with a taper and follow up. Patient concomitant medication subgroups will include at least 20 subjects taking phenytoin and at least 20 subjects taking phenobarbital with a group taking AEDs other than phenytoin or phenobarbital.
L1562: **Screening Period:** up to 21 days- assessment for study eligibility
L1563: ## Titration Phase:
L1564: The titration period on stable doses of other concomitant AEDs will be 12 weeks. Increasing doses of YKP3089 will be administered (50, 100, 150 and 200mg/day) at 2-week intervals, see **Table 100**. If signs or symptoms of phenytoin or phenobarbital dose related toxicity are found at a regularly scheduled visit an additional phenobarbital or phenytoin plasma level should be obtained from a local laboratory. The dosage of phenytoin or phenobarbital can be reduced by 25-33% at the discretion of the principal investigator at that visit or when the level is available. Further reduction of phenytoin or phenobarbital during the titration phase will be allowed based on the investigator's clinical judgment of balance between adverse events and efficacy. A maximum reduction of ~2/3 of the total baseline dose of phenytoin or phenobarbital will be allowed. Prior to initiating YKP3089, subjects at US sites only will have trough concomitant AED levels (oxcarbazepine, topiramate, lamotrigine, lacosamide, clobazam, perampanel and levetiracetam only) drawn at Visit 2. Patients taking AEDs other than PHT or PB may have adjustments of cenobamate dose during the titration phase. If clinically indicated titration can be stopped below 200mg cenobamate / day but a minimum of 50mg/day is required to continue in the study.
L1565: **Table 100 C021 Cenobamate Titration Schedule**
L1566: Cenobamate mg/day
Week 1 & 2	Week 3 & 4	Week 5 & 6	Week 7 & 8	Week 9 & 10	Week 11 & 12
12.5	25	50	100	150	200
L1567: During the maintenance phase the investigator may add (except phenytoin or phenobarbital), remove, or adjust (including phenytoin or phenobarbital) the dosage of any concomitant AEDs, as clinically indicated, Table 101. Monotherapy with YKP3089 will not be allowed. In the event of poor tolerability, the investigator may instruct the subject to take the dose of study medication in the evening or divide the total daily dose into 2 doses. Subjects will follow the schedule of study visits and assessments detailed in Table 102. After 12 months of participation in the open-label treatment period, subjects will be re-evaluated. Those who are benefiting from treatment with YKP3089 may continue use of the drug at the discretion of the investigator.
L1568: For patients in the cohort taking other concomitant AEDs, the initial target dose will be 200 mg/day. If the investigator feels that a subject requires a dose that is higher than 200 mg/day, the dose can be increased to a maximum of 400 mg/day once the target dose of 200 mg/day was reached. The upward dose adjustments should occur every other week in increments of 50-mg/day. If the investigator feels that a subject requires a dose that is lower than 200 mg/day, the dose can be reduced to a minimum of 50 mg/day once the target dose of 200 mg/day was reached. The downward dose adjustments may occur weekly by 100-mg/day or 50-mg/day. However, the downward rate of change may be more rapid or slow as clinically indicated.
L1569: **Table 101 Study C021 Dose Adjustment Rules**
L1570: Cohort	Subjects taking Phenytoin or Phenobarbital	Subjects taking AEDs other than Phenytoin or Phenobarbital
Adjustments of any Concomitant AEDs	Allowed	Allowed
Adjustments of YKP3089	Allowed	Allowed
Adjustment of Phenytoin or Phenobarbital	Allowed	Not Applicable
L1571: ## **Inclusion:**
L1572: - Male or Female \ge 18 years of age with an upper age limit of 70 years
- Weight at least 40kg
- Diagnosis of epilepsy according to the ILAE Classification- established by clinical history and EEG
- Uncontrolled partial seizures and require additional AED therapy despite treatment with at least one AED within approximately the last 2 years
- Currently on stable AED treatment- receiving stable dose of 1 to 3 AEDs for at least 3 weeks prior to Visit 2
- Benzodiazepines at least once per week during the 1 month prior to Visit 1 for epilepsy or anxiety will be counted as 1 AED and must be continued unchanged throughout the study.
L1573: - Computed tomography or MRI scan performed within the past 10 years that excluded a progressive cause of epilepsy
L1574: ## Exclusion:
L1575: - History of a serious drug induced hypersensitivity reaction.
- History of a 1st degree relative with a serious cutaneous drug-induced adverse reaction
- History of serious systemic disease
- Subjects taking phenytoin must not be taking phenobarbital or primidone; subjects taking phenobarbital must not be taking phenytoin or primidone
- Subjects taking concomitant AEDs other than phenytoin or phenobarbital, must not be taking phenytoin or phenobarbital or primidone
- Subjects with clinical evidence of phenytoin or phenobarbital toxicity
- A history of nonepileptic or psychogenic seizures
- Presence of only nonmotor simple partial seizures or primary generalized epilepsies
- Any clinically significant laboratory abnormality that in the opinion of the investigator would exclude the subject from the study
- Evidence of significant active hepatic 313disease
- Active CNS infection, demyelinating disease, degenerative neurologic disease or any CNS disease deemed to be progressive.
- Any clinically significant psychiatric illness
- Presence of psychotic disorders or unstable recurrent affective disorders
- History of alcoholism, drug abuse or 313addiction
- Current use of felbamate with less than 18 months of continuous exposure
- Current or recent use of vigabatrin or ezogabine.
- History of status epilepticus within 3 months of Visit 1
- Screening laboratory test demonstrates abnormal renal function
- ABS neutrophil count less than 1500/ul.
- Clinical or ECG evidence of serious cardiac disease. uncontrolled heart failure, major arrhythmias or QTcF less than 340ms or &gt; 450ms and &gt;470 in females.
- Platelet counts lower than 80000/uL in subject treated with VPA
- A yes answer to Question 1 or 2 of the C-SSRS Ideation Section in the past 6 months or a yes answer to any of the Suicidal Behavior Questions in the past 2 years.
- More than 1 lifetime suicide attempt
- Current use of any of the following medications: clopidogrel, fluvoxamine, amitriptyline, clomipramine, bupropion, methadone, ifosfamide, cyclophosphamide, efavirenz, fosphenytoin, ethotoin, or mephenytoin.
- Presence of congenital short QT syndrome
L1576: ## Study Endpoints
L1577: ## PK:
L1578: Sparse PK blood samples will be collected to evaluate YKP3089 and relevant concomitant AED
L1579: exposure using a population pharmacokinetics approach. This will allow for the investigation of potential effects of covariates on the pharmacokinetics of YKP3089 or other concomitant AEDs. YKP3089 and other concomitant plasma concentration data collected from this study will be pooled with relevant data from other YKP3089 clinical studies for population PK analysis
L1580: ## **Safety:**
L1581: Safety will be assessed by the nature, frequency, and severity of treatment-emergent spontaneously reported AEs, dropouts due to AEs, overall dropout rates, and changes from baseline in vital signs, physical exams, clinical laboratory evaluations, C-SSRS and 12-lead ECGs, in YKP3089-treated subjects.

L1588: ## 8.9. Additional Safety Explorations
L1589: ## 8.9.1. Human Carcinogenicity or Tumor Development
L1590: ## 8.9.2. Human Reproduction and Pregnancy
L1591: Female subjects who were pregnant or lactating were excluded from enrolling in the cenobamate clinical studies. However, 10 cenobamate-treated subjects had pregnancies reported across the clinical development program. Table 103 lists each subject by study, the action taken with study drug, and the pregnancy outcome.
